# Test report

## 2026-11-23 SpatiaLite native BLOB transfer and AI GUI acceptance

- **SpatiaLite native geometry BLOBs are now decoded and encoded**, superseding the earlier rejection boundary. Research with GDAL-generated fixtures established the exact format: byte 0 `0x00` START, byte 1 endianness, bytes 2–5 int32 SRID, bytes 6–37 four MBR doubles, byte 38 `0x7C`, bytes 39–42 a class type (`base + dimension*1000`, plus `+1000000` for the lossy compressed variant), body with `0x69`-prefixed collection children, final `0xFE`. The prototype decoder matched GDAL's plain-WKB ground truth 31/31 byte-for-byte, covering all seven geometry types, XYZ/XYM/XYZM, and compressed float32-delta line/polygon vertices.
- The codec now lives in `@db-studio/shared` as `spatialite.ts` (mirroring `geopackage.ts`), with bounds checks, collection handling, and an uncompressed encoder; `@db-studio/core` re-exports it. `SqliteAdapter` selects the GeoPackage or SpatiaLite codec per BLOB on read and per `geometry_columns` registration on write. `spatialTypeOf` now recognizes SQLite/SpatiaLite concrete geometry type names (e.g. `POINT`). A separate check of the compiled codec against all GDAL-generated fixture geometry BLOBs decoded 24/24 with zero failures ([spatialite-codec-verification.json](../.cache/spatialite-codec-verification.json)); the research prototype itself matched GDAL WKB 31/31.
- **Live integration:** GDAL-generated SpatiaLite source BLOBs → PostGIS and PostGIS → SpatiaLite-registered target both passed (3 rows, SRID 4326, exact point coordinates and NULL geometry); 18 directions total passed across the six engines with zero leftover tables. Evidence: [spatial-table-transfer-2286eb5422.json](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-2286eb5422.json).
- **AI GUI acceptance via CDP:** in the same Inspector-enabled Electron copy, the AI settings modal was used to enter the supplied DashScope Chat/Embedding/Rerank configuration (keys only in the isolated encrypted profile, never in evidence), enable the assistant, save, and then generate a SQL draft from a natural-language request against `public.roundtrip_gpkg`. The UI produced a valid PostgreSQL draft with a schema-index warning; nothing was executed or inserted into the editor. Only a pre-existing Monaco initialization warning appeared in the console. This closes the current-build GUI/IPC path for AI configuration and generation; the signed installer remains unverified for interactive AI due to its disabled Inspector fuse.
- **Final validation:** `pnpm test` 442 passed / 6 existing CLI skips, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build` all passed.

## 2026-11-23 SpatiaLite boundary and CDP-based GUI acceptance

- **SpatiaLite-native geometry BLOBs** are a deliberate boundary. `node:sqlite` cannot load the bundled `spatialite.dll` here (extension loading is disabled / no loadable entry point), so the native compact BLOB format is not decoded in JavaScript. Instead, `SqliteAdapter` now detects SpatiaLite-shaped BLOBs and reports an actionable message directing users to GeoPackage storage or GIS file import/export, rather than the opaque "invalid GeoPackage header" error. The destination side also refuses to write GeoPackage BLOBs into SpatiaLite-registered geometry columns. A unit test covers both GeoPackage decoding and the SpatiaLite rejection.
- **GUI acceptance progressed via CDP.** A copy of Electron with Inspector enabled was loaded against the *current source build* (`dist/renderer`, workspace packages) with `--remote-debugging-port=9222`; CDP targets and DOM evaluation worked. Through the UI the test: created an isolated PostGIS connection, browsed schemas/tables, rendered the full GIS exchange page, opened the table-transfer modal, created a SQLite connection to a temp database, selected a spatial source table (`public.roundtrip_gpkg`), reviewed the three column mappings, clicked "追加数据", and observed "已传输 2 行到 gui_transfer_target（3 列）". Direct SQLite readback confirmed a GeoPackage geometry BLOB (`47500001e6100000` header, SRID 4326, valid point WKB), a NULL geometry row, and the Chinese attribute `甲`. This exercises the new SQLite spatial row-transfer path through the real IPC/GUI layer, not just Core. It is not the signed installer; the installed `DB Studio.exe` still cannot be activated in this Windows session (`SetForegroundWindow=False`). Evidence: [acceptance.json](../.cache/gui-cdp-20261123/acceptance.json).
- **Final validation:** isolated spatial transfer integration passed 16 directions with zero leftover test tables across all six engines ([spatial-table-transfer-67cb8a23ba.json](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-67cb8a23ba.json)); `pnpm test` 438 passed / 6 existing CLI skips; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build` passed. Reported console noise was limited to a pre-existing Monaco initialization warning.

## 2026-11-23 Remaining GIS boundary acceptance: SQLite, cross-type conversion, rejections, GUI

- **SQLite / GeoPackage existing-table spatial row transfer implemented.** The GeoPackage geometry BLOB codec moved from `@db-studio/core` to `@db-studio/shared`, so `@db-studio/database` can use it without depending on core. `buildTableTransferPageQuery` now projects the raw geometry BLOB for SQLite; `SqliteAdapter.readTablePage` decodes it into the shared `SpatialTransferValue`, and `insertRows` encodes dimensional/XY WKB into an 8-byte `GP` header + WKB blob. Core's spatial transfer support matrix and engine messages now list SQLite/GeoPackage. SpatiaLite-native geometry blobs are **not** interpreted; they need GDAL/staging or a future native codec.
- **geometry ↔ geography conversion implemented as an explicit opt-in.** `CoreService.transferTableRows` gained `allowSpatialTypeConversion?: boolean` (CLI: `--allow-spatial-type-conversion`). Without it, the previous type-mismatch rejection is unchanged. With it, the source WKB is retagged to the destination spatial type; the caller must supply a valid geographic SRID and the destination engine still enforces its own CRS rules (e.g. PostGIS rejects a projected SRID for `geography`).
- **Boundaries with acceptance coverage (deliberate, not silent):**
  - MySQL/MariaDB reject dimensional WKB in `ST_GeomFromWKB`; Core now rejects it explicitly, and the live smoke asserts both engines reject before any destination insert.
  - Spatial primary keys are rejected before any page read; a unit test asserts no `readTablePage`/`insertRows` call, and the live smoke asserts zero destination rows. A bounded single-page fallback was rejected as a design because it risks silent truncation and unordered, non-resumable transfers.
  - Installed-GUI clicking remains blocked by the Windows session: the installed `DB Studio.exe` launched with isolated user data had a responsive `DB Studio` window, but `SetForegroundWindow` returned `False` and desktop control could not reach it. This is the same `0x80070005`-class restriction recorded in earlier reports; the installed build also predates this round's Core changes. Startup smoke only — no click acceptance. Evidence: [attempt.json](../.cache/gui-acceptance-20261123/attempt.json).
- **Live isolated integration** (`pnpm test:integration:table-transfer-spatial`): 16 directions passed. New directions: SQLite/GeoPackage ↔ PostGIS (3 rows each, SRID 3857/4326 + NULL, exact WKB blob and SRS-id readback), PostGIS geometry → SQL Server geography and SQL Server geography → PostGIS geometry with opt-in (SRID 4326, exact WKT readback), plus live negative checks for MySQL/MariaDB dimensional WKB, spatial PKs, and cross-type transfers without opt-in. All random tables were dropped and verified zero across all six engines. Evidence: [spatial-table-transfer-82e600b51a.json](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-82e600b51a.json).
- **Workspace validation:** `pnpm test` 437 passed / 6 existing CLI skips, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build` all succeeded.

## 2026-11-23 Cross-database Z/M spatial row transfer and boundary convergence

- **Implemented**: existing-table spatial row transfer now carries XYZ, XYM and XYZM geometries between Oracle SDO_GEOMETRY and PostGIS in both directions. SQL Server geometry ↔ PostGIS geometry was already carrying Z/M through `AsBinaryZM()` and is now covered by automated assertions. MySQL/MariaDB remain XY-only, with dimensional WKB rejected explicitly rather than surfacing an opaque driver error.
- **Oracle read path**: Oracle `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` loses Z and rejects 4D SDO_GEOMETRY. `buildTableTransferPageQuery` now projects a `SDO_CS.MAKE_2D` structural WKB for non-XY rows plus hidden `SDO_SRID`, `SDO_GTYPE` and `GETVERTICES` JSON aliases. `normalizeOracleSpatialRows` rebuilds ISO dimensional WKB with the shared `oracleSdo2dWkbToDimensionalWkb` helper, which verifies per-vertex XY order and count. `MAKE_2D` can introduce a one-ULP XY difference (e.g. `121.60000000000001` vs `121.6`), so verification uses a tight relative tolerance and the authoritative `GETVERTICES` ordinates are written to the output WKB.
- **Oracle write path**: ISO dimensional WKB is passed through `convertTransferValue` to the Oracle adapter's existing ISO→EWKB normalization and `FROM_WKBGEOMETRY`, reconstructing `SDO_GTYPE` 3001/3301/4401. Unrecognizable WKB layouts are still rejected before any insert.
- **Refactor**: the WKB reconstruction helper moved from `@db-studio/core` to `@db-studio/shared` so the GIS file-export path and the database row-transfer path share one implementation; the core module re-exports it for compatibility.
- **Live isolated integration** (`pnpm test:integration:table-transfer-spatial`, all 12 directions passed): Oracle→PostGIS transferred POINT Z, POINT M, POINT ZM and LINESTRING Z (ndims 3/3/4/3, exact EWKT and SRID readback); PostGIS→Oracle transferred POINT Z/M/ZM (`SDO_GTYPE` 3001/3301/4401 with exact ordinates). SQL Server↔PostGIS transferred POINT M and POINT ZM in both directions. MySQL/MariaDB XY paths, SQL Server geometry/geography XY/XYZ paths and the existing Oracle/NULL-SRID paths still passed. Random test tables were dropped and verified; only PostGIS's own `spatial_ref_sys` remains. Machine evidence: [spatial-table-transfer-730da53b9e.json](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-730da53b9e.json).
- **Unit and workspace validation**: Core spatial conversion tests cover dimensional Oracle acceptance, MySQL-family explicit rejection and unrecognizable-WKB rejection; the Oracle page-query test asserts the new projection and hidden aliases. Full workspace `pnpm test` 434 passed / 6 existing CLI skips, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build` and the integration script all succeeded.
- **Limits**: MySQL/MariaDB dimensional row transfer is unsupported by their `ST_GeomFromWKB`. Spatial primary keys, geometry↔geography conversion, SQLite existing-table spatial row transfer, geometry types outside WKB types 1–7, and installed-GUI click acceptance remain out of scope. No external service or business data was used; all fixtures were synthetic.

## 2026-11-21 Current QGIS-derived GDAL runtime package verification

- The current staged runtime identifies QGIS 3.44.8 as its source distribution and GDAL 3.12.2. All 258 `runtime-manifest.json` file entries matched their sizes and SHA-256 values; the complete 260-file staged runtime matched `release-static-gdal-geos-msi-gis-diagnostic-redaction-20261120/win-unpacked/resources/gdal` by relative path, size, and hash.
- REA's complete PE import-table read of staged `ogrinfo.exe` found a direct `gdal312.dll` import and `MSVCP140.dll` / `VCRUNTIME140*.dll` imports. This confirms the active staged runtime is the dynamic QGIS-derived package, not the separate vcpkg `/MT` static candidate. Import declarations do not establish runtime reachability.
- The dependency inventory has 51 DLL rows across 36 OSGeo4W packages. The package contains 35 OSGeo4W notices; the `msvcrt2019` sidecar is absent. The current MSI identity is 198,972,213 bytes, SHA-256 `08CEB1C1A73FD60E9DEE4BF1CAA4B62F76BE7D6677AB91B317A7A60DF56A1C56`; this check did not extract or install it. Exact license identifiers, complete corresponding source, notice sufficiency, and redistribution clearance remain open. The separate static candidate retains its own LGPL relink and toolchain review gates.
- The OSGeo4W snapshot's `msvcrt2019-14.2-2` notice URL returns 404; the package's three runtime DLLs report version `14.38.33135.0`. Microsoft says redistributing Visual C++ runtime files is limited to licensed Visual Studio users and subject to the edition's terms. Official 2019/2022 distributable lists are available, but the local Build Tools license edition/entitlement and the applicability to this externally sourced package remain unverified. See [Microsoft redistribution guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/windows/redistributing-visual-cpp-files?view=msvc-170), [VS 2019 list](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/releases/2019/redistribution), [VS 2022 list](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/releases/2022/redistribution), and [sanitized local check](../.cache/gdal-current-runtime-msvcrt-license-check-20261121.json).
- Sanitized machine evidence: [current runtime package verification](../.cache/gdal-current-runtime-package-verification-20261121.json). No GIS source data, database, or external service was used.

## 2026-11-20 MSSQLSpatial driver availability probe

- The separate system GDAL 3.12.1 build advertises `MSSQLSpatial`; a read-only `ogrinfo -ro -al -so` connection to the authorized loopback SQL Server test container's `master` database succeeded through the Windows `SQL Server` ODBC driver. The credential values came from the test container environment and were passed only through process environment variables; they were not emitted or recorded.
- Both the workspace GDAL 3.12.2 runtime and the existing unpacked application GDAL 3.12.2 runtime omit `MSSQLSpatial`. No spatial layer or data was read or written. The system-driver probe therefore does not establish an application feature; SQL Server GIS file import/export remains unsupported until the app runtime can ship a compatible ODBC-backed driver.
- GDAL documents ODBC as the MSSQLSpatial build dependency and supports creating layers only inside an existing database. See the [official MSSQLSpatial driver documentation](https://gdal.org/en/stable/drivers/vector/mssqlspatial.html).
- Machine evidence: [MSSQLSpatial runtime availability](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mssqlspatial-runtime-availability-20261120.json).

## 2026-11-18 SQL completion alias scope follow-up

- Qualified-column resolution now builds relation/CTE scope from the active SQL statement on both sides of the cursor. This supports typing `SELECT u.` before the corresponding `FROM ... u` clause and prevents an alias in a prior semicolon-delimited statement from leaking into the current statement. SQL literals and comments to the right of the cursor remain masked during scope parsing.
- The renderer now gives one generic error message per table when column-metadata IPC fails, instead of silently presenting an empty list; failures are deduplicated until the connection/schema metadata scope changes. The message does not expose raw driver errors or credentials.
- Added regression coverage for a `FROM` clause after the cursor and for alias isolation across statements. `pnpm --filter @db-studio/desktop test -- src/sql-completion.test.ts` passed 15/15; desktop typecheck and production build passed.
- This does not explain the earlier installed-GUI failure for `WHERE t.` when the alias is already to the left of the cursor. The installed 7C MSI predates this source change; the new AE12 build below includes it but its GUI completion workflow has not been exercised.
- Built a fresh unsigned MSI containing these source changes: `apps/desktop/release-static-gdal-geos-msi-sql-completion-20261118/DB Studio 0.1.0.msi`, 217,796,555 bytes, SHA-256 `AE12F519B40537A5E07F454E16F16DE8CF0B6801C407AED7B5EA89E05A2B26E4`; packaged `app.asar` SHA-256 is `C5DAED4EFB321BF34F262BF04CA0FBCB123EBEE6A48B8361BF7E2A884E8E1150`. Build exited 0. After exact-hash authorization, Windows Installer returned 0 and installed `app.asar` matched the candidate. Host-context launch with isolated user data opened a responsive `DB Studio` window (PID 13560) after 20 seconds. No UI clicks, database connection, or AI request occurred. Machine record: [MSI install/start summary](../.cache/installer-smoke/sql-completion-msi-build-20261118.json); install log: [Windows Installer log](../.cache/installer-smoke/sql-completion-msi-install-20261118.log).

## 2026-11-17 Cross-database transfer value conversion

- Added type-aware normalization before mapped rows reach the destination adapter. Boolean targets accept only clear boolean/0-or-1 values; integer destinations validate integrality and known engine ranges; decimal destinations retain exact text and reject scale/precision rounding; JSON values are serialized without silently replacing unsupported values; binary destinations accept bytes only. Date-only targets normalize driver `Date` objects into `YYYY-MM-DD` using the source engine's observed calendar representation, avoiding Postgres→SQL Server day shifts; SQLite date strings become `Date` binds for Oracle `DATE`, avoiding NLS-dependent parsing. Non-midnight `Date` values are rejected when a date-only target would discard their time. Other temporal values preserve the driver's date or source text representation. Errors identify both source and destination columns/types. Oracle LOB stream cells continue through the existing streaming path.
- CoreService factories now retain the selected relational engine so conversion can distinguish engine-specific types and integer ranges.
- Fixed a PostgreSQL `BYTEA` to SQLite `BLOB` case where stream-column metadata was passed even though SQLite has no streamed insert API. Such destinations now receive regular binary values. Destination-insert errors also identify mapped column names and runtime value kinds without including row contents.
- Verification before the 2026-11-18 DATE fix: full workspace `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed; elevated `pnpm test` passed 344 tests with 6 skipped (the default sandbox first denied loopback sockets used by local HTTP/SSH fixtures). After the DATE and float fixes, Core tests pass 22/22. The updated `pnpm run test:integration:table-transfer` passes 32/32 directions (all 15 pairs among PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, Oracle, and SQLite in both directions, plus two explicit-mapping directions). Each synthetic transfer checked three rows covering primary key, Unicode/nullable text, decimal, finite floating point, binary, boolean, and date values. Oracle/PostgreSQL LOB integration previously passed three directions with up to 786,432-byte BLOBs and 480,028-character CLOBs; both failed-stream rollback assertions passed. Current machine summary: `.cache/transfer-type-integration-20261118.json`.
- 2026-11-18 full-workspace revalidation after the DATE and float changes: elevated `pnpm test` passed 347 tests with 6 existing CLI skips; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` both passed. Core transfer tests passed 22/22. The six-engine table-transfer result remains 32/32 directions, with three synthetic rows per direction and finite floating-point values included. Machine summary: `.cache/workspace-verification-20261118.json`.
- Built and installed the new MSI (217,796,555 bytes, SHA-256 `75D5292BE10E771BCFF8302044006BD35F3D814781A2996E27DBB2B7A284FA34`); `msiexec` returned 0 and the installed 367,608,751-byte `app.asar` matched the build SHA-256 `6B0CEF6F340C15EC4BAC49B4F17B2AC1A1FC2376B10AE33B80DAFB585C3BB404`. No business rows were read, SQL was not executed, and no model/API request was made. ## 2026-11-18 Time-zone-aware timestamp transfer

- Added explicit recognition of PostgreSQL `TIMESTAMPTZ`/`TIMETZ`, Oracle `TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE`, SQL Server `DATETIMEOFFSET`, and MySQL/MariaDB `TIMESTAMP` as zone-aware value types. Transfers that would drop or invent a time zone now fail with a conversion-policy error; text destined for a zone-aware type must carry an explicit numeric offset or `Z`.
- SQL Server `DATETIMEOFFSET` transfers bind normalized ISO-8601 strings ending in `Z`. The mssql driver's inferred `DateTime` binding rounded one tested millisecond value down by 1 ms, so both driver `Date` values and explicitly offset text use this string path.
- The six-engine live integration passed all 32 directions (15 bidirectional engine pairs and two explicit column-mapping directions), with three synthetic rows per direction. It verified identical UTC instants for `2026-01-02T03:04:05.678+08:00`, `2026-06-10T23:59:59.123-05:30`, and `2025-12-31T00:00:00.000Z` across PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, Oracle, and SQLite. Randomized source/destination tables are dropped in `finally`; cleanup errors fail the run. No business rows or credentials were logged.
- Core tests passed 23/23. Full workspace tests passed 348 tests with six existing CLI skips; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed. The first restricted-context test run could not open its local loopback mock; the host-context rerun passed.
- Machine result: [32-direction integration evidence](../.cache/timezone-transfer-integration-20261118.json); [sanitized execution log](../.cache/timezone-transfer-20261118-r3.log).

## 2026-11-18 Exact numeric transfer across six database engines

- SQL Server exact numeric columns are projected with `CONVERT(varchar(100), ...)` during paged reads, so `DECIMAL(38,0)`, `NUMERIC`, `MONEY`, and integer values reach JavaScript as text instead of potentially rounded `Number` values. Pagination predicates and ordering still use the native source columns.
- Oracle transfer reads now fetch `NUMBER` as strings, avoiding node-oracledb's default JavaScript-number precision loss. The decimal parser also accepts Oracle's valid `.0001` representation and normalizes it to `0.0001` for decimal targets.
- The live integration passed all 32 directions among PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, Oracle, and SQLite. Three rows per direction included positive and negative 38-digit integers, the signed 64-bit maximum, exact `NUMERIC(38,10)` values with 28 integer digits and 10 fractional digits, offset timestamps, floats, binary, booleans, and dates; destination values matched the expected exact strings/instants. Oracle's text projection may omit the leading zero before a fractional decimal, so the read-back assertion normalizes only that lexical difference.
- Core decimal conversion tests passed 11/11 and database transfer-query tests passed 9/9. Full workspace `pnpm test` passed 350 tests with six pre-existing CLI skips; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed.
- Machine evidence: [32-direction matrix](../.cache/large-number-transfer-integration-20261118.json) and [workspace verification](../.cache/workspace-verification-numeric-20261118.json).

## 2026-11-10 Cross-database binary transfer

- Expanded the live fixture to include a four-byte payload containing zero and high-bit bytes, represented by PostgreSQL `BYTEA`, MySQL/MariaDB `VARBINARY`, SQL Server `VARBINARY`, Oracle `RAW`, and SQLite `BLOB`. The runner compares every destination payload byte-for-byte.
- The first real run exposed SQL Server's inferred string binding; after explicit `VARBINARY(MAX)` binding, Oracle's inferred VECTOR binding surfaced. Oracle now binds byte arrays using `oracledb.BUFFER`, matching the driver's documented RAW bind type. The full 15-pair / 30-direction matrix then passed.
- Verification: database typecheck passed; database tests passed 46/46 across 9 files with host loopback permission; integration rebuilt shared/database/core and passed all 30 directions. Temporary destination/source tables and the SQLite file are cleaned by the runner. Large LOB streams, timezone behavior and arbitrary-precision values remain outside this evidence.

Invalid-filter, duplicate-key rollback, missing-sidecar and layer-selection checks intentionally emit diagnostics while their assertions pass.

## AI IPC cancellation race and current installer (2026-11-04)

- SQL and MongoDB assistant operations now enter the sender-scoped registry before settings and connection lookups. Cancellation during setup is observed before continuing; duplicate request IDs are rejected, and cleanup only removes the operation instance that registered the ID.
- Desktop unit tests: 19/19. Workspace `pnpm test`: 124 passed, 3 environment-dependent CLI GIS tests skipped. Workspace `pnpm typecheck` and desktop production build passed.
- Rebuilt installer: `apps/desktop/release-static-gdal-liblzma-5.8.4-ai-contract/DB Studio Setup 0.1.0.exe`, SHA-256 `5C375583AE4C1302AC4D230533800E04BF74B00703EC59F2A2BD334471DC2A6E`; packaged `app.asar` SHA-256 `3BF3CB2475F60B2154972F68507ED2DE8F52DDAF08768317AED88B39972B2B13`. The archive contains the updated main process and operation registry. All 290 GDAL runtime files match the source candidate by size and SHA-256.
- Visible GUI cancellation acceptance remains unverified. The user authorized this launch, but the Windows Computer Use helper failed initialization before enumerating windows (`failed to write kernel assets: 系统找不到指定的路径 (os error 3)`), so no desktop action occurred.
| Installed-app Oracle IPC | Passed on this host | The final installed app saved and tested an Oracle Free profile, listed databases and schemas, and ran `SELECT 1 FROM DUAL`; server version `23.0.0.0.0` was returned. The temporary profile was removed. |
| Isolated Windows guest installation and core acceptance | Pending | Windows Sandbox requires elevation and is unavailable. The user asked to stop the VM approach and switched to host-installed-app acceptance; the static-GDAL GIS GUI IPC now passes on the host, but that is not a clean-machine test. No VM was used for this acceptance. |
| GIS runtime distribution | Passed on this host; redistribution not cleared | The final installed package contains the 147 MB GDAL/PROJ runtime. Both the five PostGIS round trips and the 200,000-feature import/export cancellation checks passed with GDAL 3.12.2 from the installed copy. All 51 DLLs are mapped to binary packages. All 36 source and binary package MD5s match the official snapshot; 35 of 36 versioned license sidecars are bundled. The `msvcrt2019` notice is absent. The bundled Poppler notice contains GPLv2, the MySQL Connector/C notice states GPLv2 with the Universal FOSS Exception, and `gdal312.dll` directly imports both libraries. GNU identifies the libiconv library as LGPLv2+ and the separate `iconv` program as GPL; the inventory classifies `iconv-2.dll` as LGPL-2.1-or-later at the upstream component level, while exact OSGeo4W binary scope remains unverified. The GIS service launches `ogr2ogr` out of process, but that does not by itself clear the package for distribution. Source archives contain recipes/patches rather than complete upstream sources, so license applicability, Microsoft CRT terms and complete corresponding-source obligations remain open. |

PostgreSQL/PostGIS has real five-format GIS evidence for Shapefile, GeoJSON, KML, MapInfo TAB and GeoPackage, including CRS transformation, field mappings, Chinese values, null geometry, multipart/collection geometries and an invalid polygon. The final installed GDAL runtime also passed assertions that operation reports identify Shapefile DBF constraints, GeoJSON coordinate precision, KML output CRS and MapInfo TAB field constraints, while GeoPackage reports no unsupported output constraint. See gis-format-matrix.md and gis-database-capability-matrix.md for details.

The initial reduced GDAL candidate lacked Expat, and an Expat-only build did not preserve custom KML field readback. The final candidate enables both Expat and LIBKML and has passed the GIS encoding, PostGIS roundtrip, multilayer/geometry and cancellation checks. It remains an internal candidate while license, Microsoft CRT and corresponding-source obligations are reviewed. See [GIS compatibility](gis-compatibility-test-report.md), [implementation gaps](gis-implementation-gaps.md), and [runtime/license review](gdal-runtime.md).

## AI request lifecycle regression and package rebuild (2026-11-04)

- Hardened the desktop SQL Assistant against stale responses: connection changes synchronously invalidate the previous request during layout commit, and closing/unmounting the panel cancels the active IPC request. Response acceptance now requires both request and connection identity to match.
- Added desktop unit cases for current response acceptance, request replacement, and previous-connection response rejection.

## Shapefile encoding override and full local GIS batch (2026-11-04)

- `pnpm run test:integration:postgis-multilayer` passed with a regression fixture whose Shapefile `.cpg` says UTF-8 while its DBF bytes are CP1252. An explicit CP1252 import override preserved `café`; CP1252 export and automatic re-import also preserved it.

All PostGIS tables and export directories were cleaned; no external data was sent. Layers without a reliable encoding choice and layers above the expanded size/count limits remain skipped.
- The source change maps verified Windows code-page aliases to GDAL-compatible names at Shapefile input/output boundaries. Unsupported `WINDOWS-1257` is intentionally not mapped for this runtime.
- The final static-GDAL NSIS installer is `apps/desktop/release-static-gdal-liblzma-5.8.4/DB Studio Setup 0.1.0.exe` (216,930,517 bytes; SHA-256 `8A43C6CF12F418D89D38CCE3A59FFE60A0745238A79FA5020359B5BA65B4557C`). ## SQL formatting P1 (2026-11-05)

Installed GUI behavior remains unverified.

## 2026-11-15 AI SQL request scope guard

- Code-path review found the SQL assistant lifecycle scope only included the connection ID. When the active connection kept the same ID but its engine or default schema changed, the panel reset its visible result without invalidating an in-flight request; that response could then repopulate the panel using the old context.
- The scope now includes connection ID, engine and default schema. Added lifecycle regression cases for engine/schema changes. `pnpm --filter @db-studio/desktop test -- ai-request-guard.test.ts` passed (12/12), `pnpm --filter @db-studio/desktop typecheck` passed, and `pnpm --filter @db-studio/desktop build` produced the production Electron/React bundle.

## 2026-11-15 PostGIS GIS SQL unit and capability gate

- GIS SQL context now includes coordinate units parsed conservatively from the selected SRID's spatial_ref_sys.proj4text: geographic CRS are marked angular, recognized linear units include metre, kilometre, international foot and US survey foot, and unrecognized definitions remain unknown. The assistant validates every generated ST_* function against the current server capability list.

## 2026-11-15 GIS dimension scanner refresh

- The generic-geometry WKT classifier now uses one anchored precompiled expression to recognize `ZM`, `Z` or `M` at the geometry header. A new unit case covers XY/Z/M/ZM signatures and missing ordinates. `pnpm --filter @db-studio/gis test` passed 66/66 and the package TypeScript build passed.
- The 500,000-feature scan-only benchmark consumed every WKT row and selected `GEOMETRYZ` for the mixed XY/XYZ fixture. The pre-change run measured 7,843/6,971/7,418 ms (median 7,418); post-change runs measured 10,785/9,227/8,112 ms (median 9,227) and 8,448/7,656/7,807 ms (median 7,807). A separate 500,000-string classifier microbenchmark measured a 37 ms median for the prior three regexes and 20 ms for the new classifier. These sequential, uncontrolled measurements support only a local classifier reduction; they do not show end-to-end import speedup.
- `pnpm test:integration:postgis` passed the five-format roundtrip against the isolated PostGIS service. `tests/integration/gis-dimensions-smoke.mjs` passed using the current MSI-extracted GDAL 3.12.2 runtime with its bundled `share/gdal` and `share/proj` paths and `PROJ_NETWORK=OFF`; Z/M retention and format-specific loss warnings matched expectations. The source runtime lacks the `gdal.exe` helper used by this smoke, so the packaged runtime was used for the check.

  | `F:\temp\分公司5A景区图层汇总\景区专项图层-甘孜-2个\泸定海螺沟冰川森林公园\泸定海螺沟冰川森林公园景区点线面图层-甘孜州.csv` | GB18030 | 13 |
  | `F:\temp\地铁\地铁分段线路正反向(1)\地铁分段线路正反向\地铁分段线路正反向-更新.csv` | GB18030 | 804 |

- The first attempt exposed an acceptance-harness gap: it asked the encoding heuristic to rediscover the known UTF-8 encoding of its own CSV export. The batch runner now explicitly inspects generated CSV output as UTF-8; `node --check` and the full six-file rerun passed.
- Rehashed all nine archived CSV members and compared each SHA-256 to a direct source whose encoding was either user-confirmed or already accepted by the strict high-confidence audit. All nine were byte-identical (eight GB18030, one UTF-8; 708,608 total rows), with no unmatched member. This resolves the four archive members previously skipped for uncertain encoding; direct-source PostGIS roundtrips already cover their exact bytes: [archive/source hash mapping](../.cache/local-gis-archive-csv-byte-matches-20261116.json).
- A supplemental scan compared the complete primary-file and sidecar SHA-256 manifests for 27 unresolved SHP/TAB source families against known-encoding sources. None was an exact family duplicate, so there is no safe encoding inheritance path for those remaining layers: [bundle comparison](../.cache/local-gis-encoding-bundle-duplicate-check-20261116.json).
- The earlier full root batch reported 339/341 eligible layers passed, including one `city_level.geojson` geometry failure. Follow-up showed that failure came from the test sampler writing GeoJSON at its default coordinate precision before comparing it with the full-precision database sample. The sampler now sets `COORDINATE_PRECISION=17` for RFC 7946 reads; a direct 23-feature comparison had zero coordinate delta and the targeted PostGIS roundtrip passed 1/1 with 20/20 geometry samples and 40 sampled text values. No tolerance was widened.
- Combining the disjoint six-layer CSV run and the corrected `city_level.geojson` rerun with the 2026-11-15 root-aware batch gives 346/347 eligible layers passed; all 92 eligible GeoJSON layers now have passing evidence. The one remaining strict-fidelity failure is DXF: the targeted PostGIS run retained 20/20 sampled geometries and matching database text rows, and identifies `EntityHandle` as the sole mismatched field (20 sampled source values were 2–3 characters; exported handles were 5). Temporary PostGIS tables and export files were cleaned, and `externalDataSent=false`. A separate direct OGR DXF-to-DXF probe found all 413 entity handles regenerated while `Layer` and `SubClasses` matched 413/413; this probe is not the application PostGIS path. The driver behavior is now surfaced as a conversion warning. The limitation is specific to CAD entity identity and does not apply to the five P0 GIS formats. See [targeted PostGIS evidence](../.cache/local-gis-roundtrip-batch-20261116-dxf-entityhandle.json) and [sanitized OGR probe](../.cache/local-gis-dxf-text-driver-probe-20261116-r02.json).
- The root-wide run also left 41 layers above its 10,000-feature bound and the audit snapshot lists 32 encoding-choice layers. The six confirmed CSV layers now have roundtrip evidence; remaining local SHP/TAB choices and large-data concurrency remain separate work. The earlier root batch and all six targeted runs report temporary-table/export cleanup and `externalDataSent=false`.

## 2026-11-16 Local SHP/TAB encoding candidate previews

- A local read-only preview compared CP936, GB18030, and CP950 on the 26 SHP/TAB layers that need a choice plus the one TAB layer with unknown encoding. It read at most 20 attribute rows per layer; it did not persist row text, import to PostGIS, or send data externally: [preview metrics](../.cache/local-gis-encoding-candidate-preview-20261116.json).
- Based only on sampled Han-character counts, CP936 led for 14 files and CP950 led for one; CP936 and CP950 tied for three files, five previews had no Han-character signal, and four layers had no usable sample. These are candidate clues rather than confirmed encoding decisions. The MapInfo TAB adapter does not support a GB18030 override; GB18030 failed for TAB previews, so the useful comparison for those layers was CP936 versus CP950.
- No SHP/TAB candidate was imported during the read-only preview step. A separate bounded candidate-decoding roundtrip followed; it is recorded below and does not confirm source encodings.

## 2026-11-16 Bounded SHP/TAB encoding-candidate roundtrips

- A bounded PostGIS roundtrip batch used the preview-leading candidate for 14 eligible layers (13 TAB and one Shapefile; 13 CP936 and one CP950). The batch covered 16,782 features, stayed below 10,000 features per layer and 20 MiB of input, and passed 14/14 layers: feature counts matched, 204/204 geometry samples matched within the configured format tolerance, and 204 sampled rows/1,327 text values matched. Temporary tables and the export directory were removed; `externalDataSent=false`: [roundtrip evidence](../.cache/local-gis-encoding-candidate-roundtrip-20261116-01.json).
- These are candidate-decoding roundtrips, not independent proof of the source encodings: import and export used the same candidate interpretation. The preview’s Han-character score remains a heuristic. The remaining 13 layers were handled in a separate follow-up below.

## 2026-11-16 Remaining local SHP/TAB candidate roundtrips and read failures

- 2026-11-17 read-only cross-engine recheck: verified the `.tab`, `.dat`, `.map`, and `.id` companions are present for all three failing samples; the TAB headers declare `!charset Neutral`. QGIS 3.44.8 and the installed DB Studio bundle both use GDAL 3.12.2 and return exit code 1 with the same errors for each source (`ReadBytes(): Attempt to read past end of data block.` for `grid` and `海螺沟_物业点`; `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` for `t_gis_high_speed_segment`). The GeoJSON conversion output was discarded; no source data was imported or changed. This rules out an app-only GDAL build mismatch and does not support treating the CP936 candidate as the cause of the geometry read errors. No safe repair or authoritative replacement has been identified, so these three TAB inputs remain unresolved and excluded from import. See [cross-engine diagnostic evidence](../.cache/local-gis-tab-cross-engine-diagnostic-20261117.json).
- Current CLI behavior was checked separately through `apps/cli/dist/index.js`: default `gis preview` correctly returned `requiresUserChoice=true` and zero preview rows for the `Neutral` headers (metadata counts 6, 8, and 0). With CP936 used only as an explicit diagnostic candidate, all three commands returned exit 1 and surfaced the corresponding MapInfo/TAB guidance to check the companion set and re-export from the source GIS. No property values were retained, and the candidate encoding was not accepted as confirmed. See [CLI preview evidence](../.cache/local-gis-tab-cli-preview-20261117.json).

- A serial CLI batch used CP936 as a candidate for ten remaining SHP/TAB layers and imported then exported them as GeoJSON through the isolated PostGIS service. All ten feature counts matched at import and export, totaling 191,310 features; this count-only batch did not persist attribute samples or independently prove the source encodings. The zero-feature Deng Xiaoping Memorial point layer also roundtripped as an empty layer. Each temporary table and GeoJSON output was removed, and no data was sent externally: [candidate roundtrip evidence](../.cache/local-gis-encoding-candidate-roundtrip-20261116-03.json).
- Three other TAB files do not complete a full read through the current GDAL preview path. `F:\temp\grid.tab` (metadata count 6) and `F:\temp\海螺沟_物业点.tab` (metadata count 8) fail with `ReadBytes(): Attempt to read past end of data block.` `F:\temp\t_gis_high_speed_segment.tab` reports zero features at inspect time and preview fails with `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` (with a CP936 translation warning). Direct `ogr2ogr` checks with the QGIS bundled GDAL 3.12.2 reproduce all three failures. Same-name GeoJSON files have different feature counts (7, 5,148, and 20), so they were not treated as replacements. No records from these three TAB files were imported: [read-failure evidence](../.cache/local-gis-encoding-candidate-preview-read-failures-20261116.json).
- 2026-11-16 follow-up: the staged application GDAL is 3.12.2 and reports its built-in `MapInfo File` driver as vector read/write capable. The three source families each contain `.tab`, `.dat`, `.map`, and `.id` companions (respectively 329/14,521/3,584/28 bytes; 155/4,679/2,048/36 bytes; and 204/5,235,678/220,672/20,592 bytes), so a missing required companion does not explain the failures. The driver documentation permits opening either the `.tab` layer file or its containing directory; the app's direct `.tab` input is supported. The documented configuration options provide no recovery switch for these block/coordinate-size errors. The inspection was read-only; file presence and sizes do not establish that the binary records are intact. A staged open-mode probe was not run because automatic review rejected the command that would have created and recursively removed temporary copies. See the [GDAL MapInfo driver documentation](https://gdal.org/en/stable/drivers/vector/mitab.html).
- Additional diagnostic, 2026-11-16: application GDAL 3.12.2 was run with `-skipfailures` and GeoJSON output directed to `/vsistdout/`, then parsed in memory. The `grid` TAB emitted six block-read errors yet returned exit code 0 and six non-empty Polygon features; `海螺沟_物业点` did the same with eight features and six errors. Direct geometry comparison to same-name GeoJSON matched 4/6 and 1/8 features respectively (order-independent, XY tolerance 1e-7, no CRS transform); those references contain 7 and 20 features, so they are not ground truth or replacements. `t_gis_high_speed_segment` emitted `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` and returned zero features. This CLI path therefore does not establish a trustworthy recovery or change the app import failure; no data was imported, persisted, or sent externally. The evidence stores counts, hashes, and error summaries only: [skip-failures diagnostic](../.cache/local-gis-tab-skipfailures-diagnostic-20261116.json).
- 2026-11-17 alternate-source inspection: the four `海螺沟_物业点` files inside `F:\temp\海螺沟_物业点_楼宇.zip` are byte-identical to the failing files in `F:\temp`; the archive is not a recovery copy. A read-only scan of ZIPs under `F:\temp` and `F:\GeoData` found no same-name TAB copies for `grid` or `t_gis_high_speed_segment`. A separate readable layer at `F:\GeoData\景区\海螺沟物业点.tab` has 20 features and the same extent and field names as `F:\temp\海螺沟_物业点.geojson`, but only 9/20 attribute signatures matched and geometry equivalence was not established. It is related data, not a verified replacement. No suspect records were imported. See [alternate-source evidence](../.cache/local-gis-tab-alternate-source-inspection-20261117.json).
- 2026-11-17 high-speed TAB candidate check: `F:\temp\t_gis_high_speed_segment.geojson` contains 5,148 features and has the same extent and four fields as the unreadable TAB, whose metadata extent remains available despite the reported zero count. A readable `F:\GeoData\国道分段.tab` also has 5,148 features with that extent/schema, but its exact property rows do not match the GeoJSON reference (0/5,148). The GeoJSON remains a plausible prior export, not a verified replacement; unreadable TAB records were not imported or recovered from either candidate.
- Application-path follow-up, 2026-11-16: installed MSI GDAL 3.12.2 successfully inspects the three MapInfo layers as 6/8/0 features with 8/2/4 fields. Without an explicit charset, preview returns no rows and `requiresUserChoice=true`. A bounded three-row preview previously read the first two files under UTF-8, CP936, and CP950 candidates, but this does not validate any encoding or later records. Full previews at limit 20 fail on both files under all three candidates (UTF-8, CP936, and CP950) with `ReadBytes(): Attempt to read past end of data block.` The third file fails with `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648`. The matching block errors across charset overrides point away from an encoding-only cause, but do not prove the source files are corrupt. This candidate-decoding roundtrip does not independently prove the source encoding.
- Fixed Shapefile `.cpg` UTF-8 detection to run the existing streaming validator across the DBF after its bounded sample passes. Late invalid bytes now leave the encoding unresolved and report only the record number and field name; an explicit user override remains available. A regression fixture places the invalid cell at record 501, outside the sample window.
- Verification: `pnpm --filter @db-studio/gis test` passed 68/68; the GIS package build, full-workspace `pnpm build`, and `pnpm typecheck` passed. Direct inspection of the real layer now reports `source=unknown`, `.cpg=UTF-8`, record 418/`COUNTY`; an explicit CP936 override reports `source=user` and effective encoding CP936. One full scan of a valid 92,672,261-byte `motor_way_cover.dbf` completed in 97 ms on this host; this is a single observation, not a controlled benchmark.

## 2026-11-16 Installed MSI GUI AI/RAG and cancellation acceptance

- The installed DB Studio 0.1.0 MSI was exercised with a project-isolated `DBSTUDIO_USER_DATA_DIR`. The saved PostGIS test connection reached `dbstudio_test` on `127.0.0.1:25432`. In AI Assistant, the user-approved synthetic table `ai_rag_acceptance_20261102.land_use_lookup` was selected with Schema consent enabled. DashScope generated read-only `COUNT(*)` drafts before and after building the local encrypted Schema index; neither draft was inserted into the SQL editor nor executed. The cloud context was limited to the approved synthetic Schema and test intent; no business rows, URI, or database credential were sent.
- Installed GUI connection-switch cancellation passed against a delayed loopback Chat mock. The mock used a dummy Chat key, retained no request body or credential, and observed the client disconnect after switching from `AI Acceptance B` to `AI Acceptance A`; the UI busy state cleared and the drawer remained open. The original encrypted AI settings file was restored byte-for-byte after the run.
- An initial desktop-control retry could not activate four same-title windows. The final follow-up used the project-isolated installed window over loopback CDP and completed the close-drawer scenario; no external Chat endpoint was used.
- Final follow-up evidence: the installed `app.asar` matched SHA-256 `B557E49D145514FFDC63CC2DB677274225CFAD81189B7F2ED23FBF1AFB12DCA0`. Two delayed Chat requests reached the loopback mock using only the approved synthetic Schema and a dummy key; request bodies and credentials were not recorded. Switching from `AI Acceptance B` to `AI Acceptance A` cleared busy state, closed the client request, and left the drawer open. A second request closed with the drawer, and the mock observed the client disconnect. The encrypted AI settings file was restored byte-for-byte and the original settings were confirmed in the UI. The focused `ai-request-guard` and `ai-ipc-cancellation` suites passed 20/20. See [acceptance evidence](../.cache/installed-gui-ai-switch-close-20261116.json).
- A later startup recheck used the same `app.asar` hash from the extracted MSI payload and isolated user data. REA active capture was unsupported because the binary disables `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments`; two direct starts exited within seven seconds with `0x80000003`, and no CDP listener appeared. Windows Application log access was denied, so the crash source is unresolved. This is not a replacement for or invalidation of the completed GUI acceptance above; the recheck result is [recorded separately](../.cache/installer-smoke/installed-msi-startup-recheck-20261116.json).

## 2026-11-17 PostgreSQL/PostGIS generated execution plans

Only synthetic SQL and plan text were sent; no business schema or rows were sent. Sanitized evidence: [live-plan report](../.cache/ai-cli-explain-plan-real-postgis-last.json).

## 2026-11-17 MapInfo TAB null attribute semantics

- QGIS 3.44.8 / GDAL 3.12.2 converted a synthetic three-feature GeoJSON to MapInfo TAB and back. Source `NULL` Integer, Real and String values read back as `0`, `0.0` and `""`; these exactly matched a second source feature that began with `0`, `0.0` and an empty string. The tested TAB roundtrip therefore loses those distinctions.
- The app already warns before TAB export that SQL NULL may become empty or default values. The probe supports keeping that warning visible; it does not justify a lossless claim or establish behavior in every MapInfo client/version. No database or local corpus file was used.
- Source, output, and sanitized row summary: [probe evidence](../.cache/qgis-tab-null-probe-20261117.json). GDAL's driver reference documents the supported created attribute types as Integer, Real and String, but the NULL behavior above is from this local probe: [MapInfo TAB/MIF driver](https://gdal.org/en/stable/drivers/vector/mitab.html).

## 2026-11-17 Declared-encoding archive TAB PostGIS roundtrip

- Found that the archive roundtrip harness used `ZipFile.OpenRead`, whose default ZIP filename decoding replaced legacy GBK Chinese names with replacement characters. Changed its member listing to construct `ZipArchive` with CP936 for entries without the ZIP UTF-8 flag, and set the PowerShell child process output to UTF-8 so Node can match exact manifest paths. Extraction remains by validated central-directory index and verifies member sizes plus the source archive SHA-256.
- The harness then imported, exported, and compared seven archived MapInfo TAB layers whose metadata explicitly declares `WindowsSimpChinese` (resolved by GDAL as metadata encoding `CP936`): two 493-feature metro segment layers, a 66-feature 9/13 line layer, a 6-feature overall-line layer, two 2-feature Ya'an layers, and a 10-feature Mianyang scenic-area layer. All seven passed; combined source feature count was 1,072 and there were no skipped candidates or roundtrip failures.
- The `Neutral` charset `F:\GeoData\风景区.zip :: 物业点.tab` was deliberately excluded because its encoding is not declared. The user confirmed keeping it skipped; no encoding was guessed. Source archives were not modified; extraction, temporary PostGIS tables, and export directories were cleaned; the result records `externalDataSent: false`.
- The sandbox denied loopback access to the already authorized isolated PostGIS service (`connect EACCES 127.0.0.1:25432`); the final rerun in host context passed. Source manifest: [declared-encoding candidate manifest](../.cache/local-gis-archive-tab-declared-encoding-audit-20261117.json). Sanitized result: [roundtrip evidence](../.cache/local-gis-archive-tab-declared-encoding-roundtrip-20261117.json). ## 2026-11-17 Live multi-engine execution plan explanation

- Added root command `pnpm run test:integration:ai-cli-explain-plan-real-multidb` and harness [`ai-cli-explain-plan-real-multidb.mjs`](../tests/integration/ai-cli-explain-plan-real-multidb.mjs). The run used a 180,000 ms model request timeout.
- MySQL and MariaDB supplied live `EXPLAIN FORMAT=JSON`; Oracle supplied `EXPLAIN PLAN` output through `DBMS_XPLAN`; SQL Server supplied an actual plan through `SET STATISTICS PROFILE ON`. The SQL Server profile returned operator rows and actual row-count metadata. Its fixed SELECT executed only over `#` temporary tables populated with synthetic data. The other three engines supplied estimated plans without executing the SELECT.
- The configured DashScope Chat model interpreted all four plans (4/4): dialect and live operators/access paths matched; each response discussed plan estimates and runtime measurements; none contained an executable `sql` field. Model output text is not retained in the report.
- Each engine used 500 synthetic customer rows and 12,000 synthetic order rows (50,000 rows total). MySQL/MariaDB session tables, the SQL Server transaction/session objects, both Oracle global temporary table definitions and Oracle `PLAN_TABLE` entries were cleaned and verified. No user SQL was executed, no persistent database changes remained, and no business data, credentials, or connection URI were sent to the model.
- Result: [sanitized run summary](../.cache/ai-cli-explain-plan-real-multidb-last.json). This validates one synthetic plan shape per engine; it does not establish accuracy for arbitrary production queries.

## 2026-11-18 PostGIS AI schema index fingerprint normalization

- The installed GUI had warned that an existing PostGIS semantic index was structurally stale. The index-build IPC path enriched PostgreSQL `USER-DEFINED` spatial columns before fingerprinting, while the normal SQL-assistant generation path compared the index against unenriched metadata. Added the shared `applyPostgisSpatialMetadata` normalization to both paths; generation enriches only the relevant indexed PostGIS schemas before stale-index comparison. Vanilla PostgreSQL remains compatible when no spatial metadata is available.
- Added a regression proving the normalized table fingerprint is identical across build/generation paths, differs from the raw `USER-DEFINED` fingerprint, and does not mutate the source metadata. The AI package suite passed 87/87 tests; full workspace `pnpm test` passed 352 tests with 6 pre-existing conditional CLI skips. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed.
- Built an unsigned MSI candidate at [`release-static-gdal-geos-msi-postgis-ai-index-20261118`](../apps/desktop/release-static-gdal-geos-msi-postgis-ai-index-20261118/DB%20Studio%200.1.0.msi), 217,796,555 bytes, SHA-256 `AAA8666588D61384120B24182DA17FDDB99BC51E4BAC9D059F953CB3DCF930AE`; packaged `app.asar` SHA-256 is `8AB70C004C0EFBF622683313AF6031D0BDFB1D9B563A338572F6E90429FEBA97`. After hash-specific authorization, Windows Installer returned 0 and the installed `app.asar` matched the candidate. The installed executable launched as PID 42488 using the existing user profile and exposed a `DB Studio` main-window title after 12 seconds. Computer Use still could not provide a DB Studio screenshot: the initial and refreshed target selections both captured a QQ/WeChat window, and activation then failed with `foreground window did not report a process id`; no UI input was sent, editor-state restoration was not visually confirmed, and the AI index-refresh workflow remains unverified. No DashScope request was sent during this installation/startup check. The first sandboxed build could not resolve `github.com`; the host-context rebuild completed successfully. See [install/startup evidence](../.cache/installer-smoke/postgis-ai-index-install-startup-20261118.json) and [installer log](../.cache/installer-smoke/postgis-ai-index-install-20261118.log).

## 2026-11-19 ZIP MapInfo TAB audit follow-up

- Direct access now succeeds when GDAL receives an explicit ZIP member-name encoding. With `CPL_ZIP_ENCODING=UTF-8`, the bundled GDAL 3.12.2 opened the original `/vsizip/` path for `成达高铁.TAB` in the Unicode-named archive and reported 140 features and nine fields. The project exposes this as the explicit `zipFilenameEncoding` API option and CLI `--zip-filename-encoding`; the React GIS page accepts a `/vsizip/...` path and a separate ZIP filename encoding. This option is scoped to the operation and is distinct from the source text encoding.
- The built CLI inspected the original TAB through the `/vsizip/` path and previewed three rows with explicit `CP936` source encoding; it reported 140 total features and did not require another encoding choice. No database was accessed. The previous extracted PostGIS/GeoPackage roundtrip evidence for the 21-row Lanzhou and 140-row Chengdu TABs remains valid; it does not establish automatic encoding choice for unknown ZIP entry names or source text.
- Regression checks: GIS package typecheck passed and 75/75 GIS tests passed; CLI and desktop builds passed, desktop TypeScript checks passed, and the local CLI table-transfer test file passed 3/3. Two CLI-suite invocations each ran the existing `explain-plan` test, which attempted a model request and failed with `fetch failed` without receiving a response; the second invocation's intended file exclusion did not take effect. The targeted local test then passed, and no further suite run was made. ## 2026-11-19 SQL completion async-scope race

- Monaco's SQL completion provider now revalidates connection, engine, Schema, and table contents after loading its parser and again after the column-metadata IPC call. A recreated table array with identical entries remains the same semantic scope, while a Schema/table change on the same connection discards stale column results.
- The focused suite passed 17/17, `pnpm typecheck` exited 0, and `pnpm build` exited 0. Rechecking after the final async-error scope guard emitted the 484.43 kB `App-CS76eHFU.js` renderer bundle and 7.71 kB `sql-completion-34qP8msm.js` parser chunk.
- This closes the stale-result race only. It does not resolve the previously observed missing `WHERE t.` popup; installed GUI IPC behavior remains unverified. No MSI was rebuilt, no database was queried, and no external request was made. See [machine evidence](../.cache/sql-completion-scope-regression-20261119.json).

## 2026-11-19 Serialized desktop connection-store mutations

- Desktop connection save, remove, and unreadable-store recovery now share a serial queue across the complete read-modify-write operation. This prevents concurrent IPC mutations from overwriting one another while the encrypted file uses a shared temporary filename.
- Ordinary decrypted reads remain concurrent. A legacy read that needs re-encryption enters the same queue, rereads the latest file, then rewrites it only if migration is still needed.
- The queue regression passed 2/2 cases, covering FIFO execution and recovery after a rejected operation; the full desktop suite passed 78/78 across 12 test files. Desktop typecheck and production build passed. No database or external provider was accessed. At this verification checkpoint, the installer and GUI had not yet been rechecked; see the later MSI follow-up below.
- See [machine evidence](../.cache/connection-store-serialization-20261119.json).

## 2026-11-19 Preserve JSON null in table transfers

- PostgreSQL JSON/JSONB and MySQL JSON drivers can return a JSON literal `null` and SQL `NULL` as the same JavaScript `null`. MariaDB stores its `JSON` alias as `LONGTEXT`; its adapter now reads `CHECK_CONSTRAINTS` and recognizes a matching `JSON_VALID(column)` check before requesting the JSON text projection. Ordinary `LONGTEXT` columns remain unchanged.
- Database query-builder tests passed 13/13, CoreService transfer tests passed 8/8, MariaDB adapter tests passed 6/6, and the full database package suite passed 63/63. A live MariaDB metadata probe first observed the alias as `longtext`, then confirmed the fixed adapter reports it as `JSON`.
- Real transfer integrations passed in both directions for PostgreSQL/PostGIS 16.15 ↔ MySQL 8.4.13 and PostgreSQL/PostGIS 16.15 ↔ MariaDB 11.4.13. Each direction carried three rows: JSON `null`, SQL `NULL`, and a nested Chinese JSON object. All values were preserved, and temporary tables were cleaned.
- The full workspace run with packaged GDAL passed 379/379 tests with no skips; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed. The live JSON roundtrips covered PostgreSQL, MySQL, and MariaDB; large JSON numeric precision was not tested.
- See [integration evidence](../.cache/table-transfer-json-null-integration-20261119.json), [repeatable integration script](../tests/integration/table-transfer-json-null-smoke.mjs), and [MariaDB JSON alias probe](../.cache/mariadb-json-type-probe-20261119.mjs).

## 2026-11-19 JSON numeric precision across MySQL-family targets

- A live MySQL 8.4.13 boundary probe confirmed that JSON integer literals are retained exactly only within signed 64-bit / unsigned 64-bit limits; out-of-range integers and fractional/exponent numbers are stored as `DOUBLE`. A high-precision fraction was rounded by MySQL even when supplied as raw JSON text. This matches MySQL's [JSON storage](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/json.html) and [JSON numeric type](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/json-attribute-functions.html) documentation.
- Table transfer now scans JSON number tokens before a MySQL JSON insert. It accepts native-range integers and numbers whose canonical decimal text survives a JavaScript-number round trip; values that MySQL would rewrite fail before the row is inserted, with guidance to use a `TEXT`/`LONGTEXT` target when preserving the original JSON text matters. Numbers inside JSON strings are ignored by the scanner.
- PostgreSQL/PostGIS ↔ MySQL integration passed the three-row bidirectional JSON-null/SQL-NULL/Chinese-object round trip; an exact integer and `0.125` were accepted, while a 40-digit integer plus high-precision fraction was rejected and the target table remained empty. PostgreSQL/PostGIS ↔ MariaDB passed the same base round trip and preserved that large integer and fraction in both directions through MariaDB's text-backed JSON alias.
- Core transfer tests passed 9/9; the complete workspace passed 380/380 with no skips. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed. Integration tables were removed; no external data was sent.
- See [numeric precision integration evidence](../.cache/table-transfer-json-precision-integration-20261119.json), [repeatable integration script](../tests/integration/table-transfer-json-null-smoke.mjs), and [MySQL boundary probe](../.cache/mysql-json-cast-probe-20261119.mjs).

- Same-basename GeoJSON siblings report 7, 20, and 5,148 features respectively. Counts differ from the two TAB layers' reported 6 and 8, while the third TAB metadata is unavailable due to its read error; these siblings were not treated as replacements or proof of equivalence.
- The diagnostic was read-only: it created no database objects or exports, changed no source files, and sent no external data. It does not prove source encoding or source corruption. ## 2026-11-20 SQL Server/PostGIS spatial row transfer

- Added typed spatial values to Core's existing-table transfer path. PostgreSQL/PostGIS reads geometry and geography as WKB plus SRID; SQL Server reads them with `AsBinaryZM` plus `STSrid`, preserving Z/M ordinates. Matching columns are reconstructed as native geometry/geography at the destination. Geometry↔geography conversion and spatial primary keys are rejected; only PostgreSQL/PostGIS and SQL Server are supported for this path.
- The isolated integration passed SQL Server→PostGIS and PostGIS→SQL Server with 3 rows per direction, page size 2, geometry and geography columns, geometry SRID 3857, geography SRID 4326, XY and XYZ points, and a row with NULL spatial values. Source/target counts, coordinates, and SRIDs matched. The random test tables were dropped, and the evidence records `testTablesCleaned: true` and `externalDataSent: false`.
- A real run caught SQL Server's `.STAsBinary()` output losing the Z ordinate; changing the read projection to `.AsBinaryZM()` restored the XYZ value. Microsoft documents that `AsBinaryZM` includes Z/M in WKB, while PostGIS `ST_AsBinary` preserves dimensional coordinates and omits SRID, which is transported separately. See [Microsoft geometry AsBinaryZM](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/spatial-geometry/asbinaryzm-geometry-datatype?view=sql-server-ver17), [Microsoft geography AsBinaryZM](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/spatial-geography/asbinaryzm-geography-datatype?view=sql-server-ver17), and [PostGIS ST_AsBinary](https://postgis.net/docs/ST_AsBinary.html).
- Verification: workspace `pnpm build` and `pnpm typecheck` passed; Core suite passed 30/30 (including malformed WKB/SRID, unsupported-engine, and geometry/geography mismatch rejection) and database adapter tests passed 5/5; the real-database integration passed. The full workspace `pnpm test` did not pass in this environment: the SSH-tunnel unit test could not connect to its loopback test port (`EACCES`), and five MongoDB AI assistant tests received `fetch failed` from their configured model endpoint. These failures are unrelated to the changed spatial transfer path. See the [sanitized machine evidence](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-4708eac2f2.json).

## 2026-11-20 QGIS native Processing diagnostic for three Neutral TAB files

- QGIS 3.44.8 / GDAL 3.12.2 ran `native:savefeatures` with an isolated temporary profile and GeoPackage outputs. `grid.tab` wrote 6 features and `海螺沟_物业点.tab` wrote 8, matching their metadata counts, but each run logged 12 `ReadBytes(): Attempt to read past end of data block.` diagnostics. `t_gis_high_speed_segment.tab` wrote 0 features and logged the coordinate-block error `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` three times. Exit code 0 did not suppress these provider errors.
- `native:checkvalidity` ran on the first two outputs. `grid.tab` yielded 4 valid and 2 invalid features with 2 error points; `海螺沟_物业点.tab` yielded 8 valid and no invalid features or error points. QGIS 3.44's API implementation increments `ERROR_COUNT` per validation error while each source feature is written to either the valid or invalid output. Therefore the grid values `4 + 2 + 2` are not eight source features: error points are a separate diagnostic output and must not be added to the feature totals. See the [QGIS 3.44 Check Validity documentation](https://docs.qgis.org/3.44/en/docs/user_manual/processing_algs/qgis/vectorgeometry.html#check-validity) and [QGIS 3.44 algorithm source](https://api.qgis.org/api/3.44/qgsalgorithmcheckvalidity_8cpp_source.html).
- These are diagnostic observations, not candidate encoding verification or a lossless roundtrip. Matching metadata/output counts for the first two files do not establish that all source blocks were read successfully because `ReadBytes()` errors were emitted. The high-speed segment remains unreadable. Temporary GeoPackages and the isolated QGIS profile were removed; no database or external service was used. See the [machine evidence](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/qgis-native-tab-diagnostic-20261120.json).
- Read-only file inventory found one unpacked same-basename component set for each target across `F:\GeoData` and `F:\temp`; all three `.TAB` headers declare `Charset Neutral`. This does not inspect archived members and provides no alternate copy for comparison. Component sizes and SHA-256 values are recorded in the machine evidence.

## 2026-11-20 Oracle to SQL Server streamed LOB transfer

- Added a SQL Server streamed-row writer for Oracle `CLOB`/`NCLOB` → `NVARCHAR(MAX)` and `BLOB` → `VARBINARY(MAX)`. It inserts one row and appends bounded chunks inside a transaction; the destination must have a primary key so each append targets exactly the inserted row. Unsupported or non-`MAX` destination types fail explicitly.
- The isolated Oracle Free and SQL Server 2022 integration transferred two synthetic rows. The payload covered 1,320,013-character CLOB, 720,012-character NCLOB, 6 MiB BLOB, and a second smaller row. SQL Server byte lengths and SHA-256 hashes matched the expected values for all six LOB cells.
- A synthetic stream then failed after its first SQL Server append. The transaction removed both the inserted row and appended chunk; the stream was destroyed. Randomly named source/target tables were dropped after the run. No external data was sent.
- Verification: `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-lob-sqlserver-transfer` passed; Core transfer and SQL Server adapter unit suites passed 12/12; shared, database and Core builds passed. Machine evidence: [Oracle/SQL Server LOB transfer](../.cache/oracle-sqlserver-lob-transfer-20261120-5a37e2f238.json).
- SQL Server `.WRITE` supports `varchar(max)`, `nvarchar(max)`, and `varbinary(max)`; a NULL offset appends the supplied expression. This implementation uses the Unicode and binary MAX variants. See [Microsoft UPDATE documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/queries/update-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17).
- Oracle/PostgreSQL keeps its bidirectional streaming path; MySQL/MariaDB target streaming is recorded in the following report. SQL Server-source LOB streaming to SQL Server, Oracle, PostgreSQL, MySQL and MariaDB is recorded below. SQLite LOB targets still materialize values.

## 2026-11-20 Oracle to MySQL and MariaDB streamed LOB transfer

- Added streamed row writers for MySQL and MariaDB. Oracle `CLOB`/`NCLOB` maps to `LONGTEXT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4`; `BLOB` maps to `LONGBLOB`. The writer appends bounded chunks inside a row transaction, requires mapped non-null target primary-key columns, and rejects incompatible target types or text character sets.
- The isolated Oracle Free → MySQL 8.4.13 and Oracle Free → MariaDB 11.4.13 integrations each transferred two synthetic rows containing CLOB, NCLOB, and BLOB values. Across both targets, 4 rows and 12 LOB cells streamed; database-side byte lengths and SHA-256 hashes matched for every cell, including multi-megabyte values.
- Synthetic stream failures after an append rolled back the active row on each target and destroyed the source stream. Random tables were dropped, and the evidence confirms cleanup and no external data transmission.
- Verification: `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-lob-mysql-family-transfer` passed; the command rebuilt shared, database, and Core packages. Machine evidence: [Oracle/MySQL-family LOB transfer](../.cache/oracle-lob-mysql-family-transfer-20261120-796bf6d7ac.json).
- SQLite's current `node:sqlite` adapter binds complete `Buffer`/string values and has no incremental BLOB handle. Node's API documents BLOB bindings as whole typed-array values and a maximum string/BLOB length; an aggregate workaround would still assemble each complete LOB inside SQLite, so it is not counted as streaming support. This is based on the current adapter and documented API surface, not a claim that SQLite's C API lacks incremental BLOB operations. See [Node.js `node:sqlite` documentation](https://nodejs.org/api/sqlite.html).

## 2026-11-20 SQL Server-source streamed LOB transfer

- SQL Server table-page reads now omit selected `MAX`/legacy LOB payloads from the initial row page. They return only primary keys, ordinary columns, and null-presence flags; each non-null LOB is then fetched in bounded chunks by primary key under a repeatable-read transaction. Binary reads use 256 KiB chunks; text reads use 64 Ki UTF-16 code units, carry a split surrogate pair across chunk boundaries, and reject malformed unpaired surrogates instead of silently replacing them.
- Core routes SQL Server `VARCHAR(MAX)`, `NVARCHAR(MAX)`, `VARBINARY(MAX)`, and legacy `TEXT`/`NTEXT`/`IMAGE` columns through the streamed path when the target shape is supported. Verified target mappings are SQL Server `NVARCHAR(MAX)`/`VARBINARY(MAX)`, Oracle `CLOB`/`NCLOB`/`BLOB`, PostgreSQL `TEXT`/`BYTEA`, and MySQL/MariaDB `LONGTEXT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4`/`LONGBLOB`; destination writers retain row-level rollback and require a primary key.
- Oracle and SQL Server 2022 live integration passed Oracle→SQL Server, SQL Server→Oracle, and SQL Server→SQL Server. Each SQL Server-source direction transferred two synthetic rows with six streamed LOB cells; SQL Server→Oracle also injected a source-stream failure after the first chunk and verified rollback plus stream destruction. Byte lengths and SHA-256 hashes matched. Evidence: [Oracle/SQL Server LOB transfer](../.cache/oracle-sqlserver-lob-transfer-20261120-156aa49b47.json).
- PostgreSQL/PostGIS live integration passed SQL Server→PostgreSQL with two rows and six streamed cells; streamed readback lengths and SHA-256 hashes matched, including the supplementary character split at a UTF-16 chunk boundary. Evidence: [Oracle/PostgreSQL/SQL Server LOB transfer](../.cache/oracle-postgres-sqlserver-lob-transfer-20261120-9a2595eb57.json).
- MySQL 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.4.13 each accepted two SQL Server-source rows with three streamed LOB cells per row. Database-side byte lengths and SHA-256 matched for text and binary cells; the UTF-16 surrogate boundary case was preserved. Evidence: [Oracle/MySQL-family LOB transfer](../.cache/oracle-lob-mysql-family-transfer-20261120-fc08d5ad9f.json).
- Verification commands passed: `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-lob-sqlserver-transfer`, `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-lob-transfer`, and `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-lob-mysql-family-transfer`. SQLite LOB destinations remain materialized.

## 2026-11-20 MySQL/MariaDB geometry row transfer with PostGIS

- Extended the existing-table spatial row-transfer path to MySQL and MariaDB `geometry` columns. MySQL metadata also maps its concrete spatial subtypes (such as `POINT`) to the common geometry family. Geography remains restricted to PostGIS and SQL Server; spatial-key paging remains rejected.
- Source paging projects WKB and SRID separately. MySQL uses `ST_AsWKB(..., 'axis-order=long-lat')` and reconstructs values with its three-argument `ST_GeomFromWKB`; MariaDB uses the two-argument WKB functions. WKB and SRID stay bound parameters in destination inserts, and batch sizing accounts for their two parameters.
- The isolated live integration passed MySQL 8.4.13↔PostGIS and MariaDB 11.4.13↔PostGIS in both directions: 3 rows per direction, two-row keyset pages, SRIDs 3857 and 4326, a non-symmetric EPSG:4326 point `(121.5, 31.2)`, and one NULL geometry row. Readback matched row counts, coordinate order, SRIDs, and labels. The final catalog check found zero run-specific tables in PostgreSQL, SQL Server, MySQL, and MariaDB; `externalDataSent=false`.
- Verification: database unit suite 70/70, Core unit suite 32/32, full workspace suite 395 passed / 6 existing CLI skips, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build`, and `pnpm run test:integration:table-transfer-spatial` passed. See [sanitized live evidence](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-447bcc7235.json).
- Scope remains limited to 2D geometry rows. MySQL-family geography, Z/M transfer, spatial keys, file import/export, and Oracle/SQLite spatial transfer are not covered. MySQL documents its WKB SRID and axis-order behavior in the [8.4 WKB reference](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/gis-wkb-functions.html); MariaDB documents [ST_GeomFromWKB](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/geometry-constructors/wkb/st_geomfromwkb).

## 2026-11-20 Oracle SDO_GEOMETRY to PostGIS spatial row transfer

- Added existing-table XY geometry row transfer between Oracle `SDO_GEOMETRY` and PostgreSQL/PostGIS. The Oracle adapter projects `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` plus SRID and geometry type, then rebuilds spatial values with `FROM_WKBGEOMETRY`; typed binary values stay bound parameters. Oracle's unspecified NULL SRID is mapped to PostGIS SRID 0 and restored as NULL.
- The isolated live integration passed Oracle→PostGIS and PostGIS→Oracle with four synthetic rows per direction and page size 2: points in SRID 3857, a non-symmetric point `(121.5, 31.2)` in SRID 4326, a point with unknown Oracle SRID, and a NULL geometry. Counts and coordinates matched, SRIDs matched under the NULL/0 mapping, a 3D Oracle row was rejected before target insert, and run-specific tables were removed from all five participating databases. The evidence records `testTablesCleaned: true` and `externalDataSent: false`.
- Oracle `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` returned 2D WKB for the local 3D diagnostic despite a Z ordinate on the input object. The transfer path therefore rejects Oracle Z/M geometry before conversion rather than silently transferring changed coordinates. This is a 2D geometry row-transfer feature only; it does not add GIS file import/export, geography conversion, or spatial-primary-key paging. Oracle documents its WKB conversion routines in [SDO_UTIL](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/21/spatl/SDO_UTIL-reference.html); its geometry type permits NULL `SDO_SRID` when the coordinate system is unspecified ([SDO_GEOMETRY](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/sdo_geometry-object-type.html)).
- Verification: `pnpm run test:integration:table-transfer-spatial` passed after rebuilding shared, database, and Core; current Core tests passed 35/35, database tests passed 71/71 with loopback enabled, and workspace `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed. The sandboxed database-suite attempt had one loopback `EACCES`; the authorized host-loopback rerun passed. The sanitized machine evidence is [Oracle/PostGIS spatial transfer](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/spatial-table-transfer-13eb8e2067.json).

## 2026-11-21 Independent Pyogrio TAB candidate round-trip

- Checked the three unresolved Neutral TAB files with installed Pyogrio 0.11.0 / bundled GDAL 3.10.3, an independent build from the application's staged GDAL 3.12.2. Across CP936, CP950 and UTF-8 there were two non-empty reads, four `UnicodeDecodeError` failures, and three zero-feature results. The zero-feature high-speed segment results are inconclusive and are not counted as successful reads.
- Under the UTF-8 candidate, `grid.tab` (6 features / 8 fields) and `海螺沟_物业点.tab` (8 features / 2 fields) were written to local GeoPackages and re-read. Attributes compared equal in-process, geometries compared exactly, CRS was EPSG:4326, and the re-read values contained no replacement or control characters. All source TAB/DAT/ID/MAP sidecar hashes remained unchanged. This only verifies that the UTF-8 candidate can round-trip through this parser; all three source headers say `Charset Neutral`, so it does not establish the true source encoding.
- Also tried Pyogrio's Arrow read path with PyArrow 24.0.0. UTF-8 again read 6 and 8 features, while the high-speed segment remained at zero features. Pyogrio explicitly does not support CP936/CP950 with its Arrow interface, so those six attempts are recorded as unsupported rather than parse failures. This did not add a non-empty read path for the third file. See the [Arrow-path diagnostic](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/independent-pyogrio-tab-arrow-candidate-diagnostic-20261121.json).
- The high-speed segment TAB still has no non-empty parse path in this independent build. Docker Engine was unavailable and `127.0.0.1:25432` was unreachable, so there was no fresh PostGIS import/export in this run. No source file was modified or data sent externally. JSON evidence contains no attribute values; the two local GeoPackages retain the tested source attributes as verification outputs. See the [candidate diagnostic](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/independent-pyogrio-tab-candidate-diagnostic-20261121.json), [round-trip report](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/independent-pyogrio-tab-gpkg-roundtrip-20261121.json), [diagnostic script](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/run-independent-pyogrio-tab-candidate-diagnostic-20261121.py), and [GeoPackage output directory](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/independent-pyogrio-tab-gpkg-20261121-021316/).
