## 2026-11-21 Reject GDAL error diagnostics with zero exit code

- Replayed all three Neutral TAB files with the staged GDAL 3.12.2 runtime. `ogrinfo -ro -al -so` exited 0 for each: `grid.tab` and `海螺沟_物业点.tab` reported metadata counts 6/8 alongside six `ReadBytes()` errors each, while the high-speed segment reported `Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` and count 0. `ogr2ogr -skipfailures -f GPKG` also exited 0 for all three; readback counts were 6/8/0. The first two outputs had matching metadata counts but their parser errors mean complete reads are unproven; the third output was empty.
- The GIS service inspects a source before import or conversion, so `ogrinfo` diagnostics now stop those operations even if the process callback reports success. The export-process path also fails on `ERROR <number>:` diagnostics even when its close code is 0; its streaming detector retains a short stderr tail so a split `ERROR N:` prefix is recognized. Original diagnostics remain in the error/report and existing MapInfo guidance is appended.
- Added regressions for the successful `ogrinfo` callback with a GDAL error and for a split `ogr2ogr` error followed by close code 0. GIS package tests passed 81/81 and GIS package typecheck passed.
- The diagnostics were read-only: all 12 TAB family component hashes matched before/after, temporary GeoPackages were removed, and no database or external service was used. These checks do not repair or recover the layers. No source encoding or corruption conclusion is made.
- See the [three-file runtime matrix](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/neutral-tab-zero-exit-matrix-diagnostic-20261121.json), the [high-speed detailed probe](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/highspeed-tab-skipfailures-diagnostic-20261121.json), [GIS service](../packages/gis/src/index.ts), and [regression tests](../packages/gis/src/index.test.ts).

## 2026-11-21 Oracle SDO 3D WKT/WKB conversion safety probe

- Started the previously authorized isolated Oracle Free test container and ran one read-only query against `DUAL` using a synthetic `SDO_GEOMETRY(3001, 4326, SDO_POINT_TYPE(121.5, 31.2, 52.5), ...)`; no table or other database object was created. `TO_WKTGEOMETRY` returned `POINT (121.5 31.2 0.0)`. The 29-byte `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` result has WKB type code 1 (2D Point) followed by three ordinates whose third value is `0.0`; it does not encode the source Z value or carry a 3D type flag.
- This local Oracle 23c Free observation confirms that neither conversion is a safe XYZ fallback for this geometry. The existing row-transfer guard continues to reject non-XY Oracle geometries before destination inserts. The result covers one synthetic Point, not every Oracle geometry class. The container was stopped again; Docker reports `Exited (137)` after its stop timeout, while the container and data volume remain. No business data or external service was involved.
- Machine evidence and runner: [`oracle-sdo-wkt-probe-20261121.json`](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-sdo-wkt-probe-20261121.json) and [`oracle-sdo-wkt-probe-20261121.mjs`](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-sdo-wkt-probe-20261121.mjs). Oracle documents `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` and `TO_WKTGEOMETRY` as standard WKB/WKT conversion routines ([WKB](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/sdo_util-to_wkbgeometry.html), [WKT](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/sdo_util-to_wktgeometry.html)); the dimensionality result above is from the local probe.

## 2026-11-21 SQL Server GIS vector export

- Added Core export for one SQL Server `geometry` or `geography` column with a uniform SRID. Rows stream in batches into a temporary GeoPackage, then GDAL writes the requested vector format. SQL Server's spatial reference catalog supplies CRS metadata; when it is unavailable, export requires an explicit source CRS. The bridge reads `AsBinaryZM()` so SQL Server Z/M ordinates are retained, and stages decimal/money values as text to avoid floating-point rounding.
- This uses Microsoft's documented [`sys.spatial_reference_systems` catalog](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/system-catalog-views/sys-spatial-reference-systems-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17) and [`AsBinaryZM()` output](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/spatial-geometry/asbinaryzm-geometry-datatype?view=sql-server-ver17), which includes carried Z/M values.
- CLI `gis export --table schema.table` and the desktop GIS export route now include SQL Server. The desktop form can inspect SQL Server attribute columns and map output names/types. SQL Server file import was added and validated in the later [GIS vector import section](#2026-11-21-sql-server-gis-vector-import).
- Against the authorized local SQL Server test service, synthetic `geometry` and `geography` tables each exported 3 rows to GeoPackage. EPSG:4326, NULL geometry, mapped field names, exact decimal text, geography extent, and XYZM coordinates `[3, 4, 5, 6]` were verified by re-reading the GeoPackage. The runner verified both random test tables and both output files were removed; it records only synthetic acceptance metadata in [machine evidence](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlserver-gis-export-20261121-c950ee44.json).
- Core tests passed 42/42 and desktop tests passed 82/82. Core/CLI/desktop typechecks and desktop production build passed. The CLI/GDAL live integration passed. This run verifies GeoPackage output, not every GDAL driver or an installed GUI click path. Custom or inaccessible SQL Server CRS catalog entries need an explicit `--source-crs`; file import is covered in the later section. The [integration runner](../tests/integration/sqlserver-gis-export-smoke.mjs) can be rerun with `pnpm run test:integration:sqlserver-gis-export`.

## 2026-11-21 MySQL and MariaDB GIS vector import

- Added a direct MySQL/MariaDB GIS file import path in Core. GDAL converts the selected source layer into a temporary GeoPackage; Core reads it in bounded batches, extracts standard WKB and the GeoPackage SRS id, creates a generic `GEOMETRY` column, and inserts rows through the existing database adapter. The GDAL subprocess receives a runtime-variable allowlist and does not inherit database credentials.
- CLI `gis import --target database.table` now routes MySQL/MariaDB connections to this path. The desktop GIS target selector accepts PostGIS, MySQL, and MariaDB for import; the existing GIS import IPC routes the selected connection to the same Core method. Export remains PostGIS-only.
- Against the authorized isolated MySQL 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.4.13 services, a synthetic two-Point GeoJSON passed create, append, and overwrite. Both databases preserved Chinese text attributes and EPSG:4326 geometries. A failed overwrite caused by an overlength field was rejected while the original four-row table remained intact. The MySQL CLI route also imported and verified two rows.
- Core tests passed 40/40; Core, CLI, and desktop typechecks passed. The integration runner verified all temporary tables were dropped and the synthetic source was removed. Evidence: [machine result](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mysql-family-gis-import-20261121.json), [runner](../tests/integration/mysql-family-gis-import-smoke.mjs), and [Core bridge](../packages/core/src/index.ts).
- This acceptance used synthetic data only; it did not import files from `F:\\GeoData` or `F:\\temp`, send data externally, or validate GUI clicks in an installed package. MySQL/MariaDB import does not yet support field mapping/type casts, spatial filters, MakeValid, UTF-8 tail repair, or spatial-index creation. Append inserts commit per batch; a failure after earlier batches can leave those rows committed and reports the committed count.

## 2026-11-21 MySQL and MariaDB GIS vector export

- Added a MySQL/MariaDB vector export path that streams one geometry column and its attributes into a temporary GeoPackage, then uses GDAL to create the requested vector output. It rejects multiple geometry columns and mixed SRIDs. Attribute mapping/type casting and an explicit source CRS for missing server metadata are supported; database credentials are not passed to GDAL.
- The latest isolated live run passed against MySQL 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.4.13. For each engine, a synthetic GeoJSON completed create (2 rows), append (4 rows), failed overwrite with the original table preserved, and successful overwrite (2 rows). Both exports produced two Unicode-bearing features in GeoPackage. MySQL resolved EPSG:4326 from its SRS catalog without an override; the MariaDB test required the explicit `EPSG:4326` override because this instance returned no `SPATIAL_REF_SYS` row for SRID 4326. MariaDB documents `ST_SRID()` as an integer association and exposes SRS definitions through `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.SPATIAL_REF_SYS` ([ST_SRID](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/geometry-constructors/geometry-properties/st_srid), [SPATIAL_REF_SYS](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/system-tables/information-schema/information-schema-tables/information-schema-spatial_ref_sys-table)).
- Both MySQL and MariaDB CLI import/export routes passed; the MariaDB CLI export supplied explicit EPSG:4326 because this instance had no matching SRS definition. Core tests passed 42/42; the desktop GIS export bridge test passed 1/1; Core and desktop typechecks passed; Core, CLI, and desktop production builds passed. The integration runner reported that all temporary test tables and source files were removed.
- Validation used synthetic data only. It did not read files under `F:\GeoData` or `F:\temp`, send data externally, or click through an installed GUI. Only GeoPackage output was exercised live; other GDAL output drivers are not covered by this database integration. Export requires a single geometry column and uniform SRID; spatial filtering was not yet implemented at the time of this dated test and was later added across five engines (see [bbox export acceptance](#2026-11-22-gis-source-crs-bbox-export-across-five-databases)). Machine evidence: [integration result](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mysql-family-gis-import-20261121.json), [runner](../tests/integration/mysql-family-gis-import-smoke.mjs), and [Core export path](../packages/core/src/index.ts).

## 2026-11-21 SQL Server GIS vector import

- Added SQL Server GIS import for `geometry` and `geography`. GDAL stages the selected vector layer as a temporary GeoPackage; Core streams attributes and WKB in batches. Geography imports require a non-zero SRID whose `sys.spatial_reference_systems.well_known_text` identifies a geographic CRS. The documented [`geography::STGeomFromWKB`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/spatial-geography/stgeomfromwkb-geography-data-type?view=sql-server-ver17) constructor consumes WKB and the supplied SRID; the server catalog exposes supported spatial references ([SRID guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/spatial/create-construct-and-query-geography-instances?view=sql-server-ver17)). CLI `--spatial-column-type geometry|geography` and the desktop import selector route to the same Core method.
- The isolated SQL Server 2022 Developer service passed synthetic GeoJSON create (2 rows), append (4 rows), and overwrite (2 rows) for both types. EPSG:4326, NULL geometry, Unicode attributes, and the source integer attribute's SQL Server `BIGINT` type/value were verified. An EPSG:3857 source was rejected for geography before rows were inserted. Duplicate create was rejected without changing the target; an overlength value caused a geometry append batch to fail and leave existing rows unchanged. The runner confirmed all temporary tables and input files were removed; [machine evidence](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlserver-gis-import-20261121-39a39649.json) records synthetic metadata only. See the [integration runner](../tests/integration/sqlserver-gis-import-smoke.mjs).
- Core tests passed 42/42, desktop tests 82/82, and CLI tests 27 passed / 6 skipped. Core, CLI, and desktop typechecks, desktop production build, and live SQL Server CLI integration passed. The CLI explain-plan test uses a local HTTP mock because the AI provider uses pinned native HTTP transport; no external model request was made.
- This live import check used synthetic points only. It did not read files under `F:\GeoData` or `F:\temp`, call an external model, or validate installed GUI clicks. Complex geography lines/polygons, other GDAL input drivers, and installed GUI interaction remain unverified. Field renaming/type casts, spatial filters, MakeValid, incomplete UTF-8 tail repair, and spatial-index creation remain unsupported. Append commits batches independently; a later failed batch can leave prior committed batches in place, although the tested failing batch rolled back without changing existing rows.

## 2026-11-21/22 Current geography MSI build, install, and startup probe

- Added a reproducible Windows MSI build target using the current bundled-preload/static-GDAL configuration and a new output directory. The desktop production build completed; WiX produced `../apps/desktop/release-static-gdal-geos-msi-geography-20261121/DB Studio 0.1.0.msi` (198,996,789 bytes, SHA-256 `8370D8114DCAE87DE525CB22C3757E7992CF96E40FBC38931B43D18E8CF39315`). The package is unsigned.
- Windows Installer recorded `DB Studio` installation completed successfully with status 0; the log also contains an Error 2911 cleanup entry, but the installed executable hash matches the built package. Repeated launches with an isolated `DBSTUDIO_USER_DATA_DIR` (including an explicit Chromium log-file path) exit with `0x80000003` (`-2147483645`) before a window appears; neither an Electron log nor the expected `session` directory is created. The installed archive contains the declared `dist/electron/main.js` entry, and its ASAR header SHA-256 matches the hash embedded in the executable, so this probe did not find an archive integrity mismatch. A 2026-11-22 PowerShell launch of the previously successful 2026-11-18 unpacked build also exited with `0x80000003`; the failure is not isolated to the current MSI, and this comparison does not establish the root cause.
- REA observed `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments=disabled`, `EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation=enabled`, and `OnlyLoadAppFromAsar=enabled` in the installed executable. `capture_electron_scenario` therefore rejected active capture for this binary. The local development Electron binary also has `OnlyLoadAppFromAsar=enabled`; launching the unpacked source with `electron .` is therefore not a valid way to distinguish a product startup failure. An earlier elevated Windows event-log query found no matching application crash event or dump; the latest non-elevated query was denied. No GUI click acceptance is claimed.
- Evidence: [machine record](../.cache/installer-smoke/db-studio-msi-geography-20261121.json), [Windows Installer log](../.cache/installer-smoke/msi-install-geography-20261121.log), and [startup record](../.cache/installer-smoke/db-studio-msi-geography-install-startup-20261121.json).

## 2026-11-22 Oracle SDO_GEOMETRY GIS file import and export

- Added `CoreService.importGisToOracle` and `exportGisFromOracle`. Both stage through a temporary GeoPackage; import supports create/append/overwrite and sends standard WKB to `MDSYS.SDO_UTIL.FROM_WKBGEOMETRY`, while export streams Oracle attributes and `TO_WKBGEOMETRY` into a GeoPackage before GDAL writes the requested format. Oracle query streaming can now use the same fetch type handler as paged reads so BLOB/CLOB values are returned as binary/text instead of live LOB handles.
- Wired the Oracle `schema.table` route into `dbstudio gis import/export` and desktop GIS Electron IPC. Import reports XY geometry support and warns that it does not register `USER_SDO_GEOM_METADATA` or build a spatial index. Export requires exactly one `SDO_GEOMETRY` column and one SRID; it reads CRS WKT from `MDSYS.CS_SRS`, or requires an explicit source CRS if Oracle cannot provide it. Unknown source SRID is represented as NULL in Oracle and SRID 0 in GeoPackage exports.
- Oracle Free live integration passed with synthetic GeoJSON containing two EPSG:4326 XY points (Chinese attributes) and one NULL geometry: create 3 rows; read back labels, values, coordinates and SRID; append to 6; overwrite back to 3; export a 3-row GeoPackage with two non-NULL geometries. A Z GeoJSON import was rejected and its failed destination removed. A hand-created Oracle `SDO_GTYPE=3001` XYZ row was rejected by export preflight before WKB conversion. All test tables and temporary inputs/outputs were cleaned; evidence records `testTablesCleaned: true` and `externalDataSent: false`.
- Verification: `pnpm run test:integration:oracle-gis-file-roundtrip` passed against the isolated Oracle Free service; workspace `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed; the full workspace unit suite passed 414 tests with 6 existing CLI cases skipped. The integration was synthetic and did not read `F:\GeoData`/`F:\temp`, contact an external model, or exercise installed GUI clicks. Oracle Z/M remains unsupported; spatial metadata registration, spatial indexes, spatial filters, field mapping/type casts on import, and real source datasets remain unverified.
- Machine evidence: [Oracle GIS file roundtrip](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-20261121.json); reproducible script: [oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs](../tests/integration/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs).

## 2026-11-22 GIS import field mapping across four database engines

- Implemented GIS import field mapping for MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, and Oracle in Core, CLI, and desktop IPC. Imports now apply mapped destination names and target types through the temporary GeoPackage staging path. Supported requested types are string, integer/integer64, real, date, time, and datetime. Date/time/datetime fields are staged as strings before database insertion to avoid SQLite/OGR time casts changing wall-clock values. Oracle time maps to `VARCHAR2(32)` because Oracle has no standalone TIME type.
- A live synthetic GeoJSON run passed against the isolated MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, and Oracle services. ## 2026-11-22 Oracle SDO spatial-index opt-in

- Oracle GIS import now accepts `createSpatialIndex` in Core, `--create-spatial-index` in CLI, and a desktop GIS checkbox. It is limited to create mode and the connected Oracle user's own schema. The table and geometry-column identifiers are uppercased and restricted to simple ASCII letters, digits, and underscores (maximum 30 characters). It requires at least one non-NULL XY geometry, a positive common SRID, and a finite staged XY extent.
- After all rows are inserted, Core registers the extent in `USER_SDO_GEOM_METADATA`, padded by 5% (with a small scale-aware minimum), and creates a `MDSYS.SPATIAL_INDEX` domain index. It queries `USER_INDEXES` and only reports success when both `DOMIDX_OPSTATUS` and `STATUS` are `VALID`. On failure it attempts to drop the index, remove its metadata, and drop the newly created table. It does not alter an existing table or support append/overwrite index creation.
- The isolated Oracle Free integration passed both Core and built CLI paths with synthetic EPSG:4326 GeoJSON containing two points and one NULL geometry. Metadata contained exactly one expected table/column/SRID row; both domain indexes reported VALID. Case normalization was exercised by passing lower-case schema/table input. Existing create/append/overwrite/export and Z/M rejection checks also passed. The test confirmed all tables, metadata, domain indexes, and temporary files were cleaned; `externalDataSent=false`.
- Verification after the implementation: `pnpm build` passed, `pnpm typecheck` passed, the host-loopback `pnpm test` suite passed 418 tests with 6 existing CLI cases skipped, and `pnpm exec node tests/integration/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs` passed against the isolated Oracle service. The sandbox-only test attempt failed only on existing loopback EACCES cases and five MongoDB assistant mock tests; rerunning with host loopback permission passed the complete suite.
- Desktop checkbox and Electron IPC compile and build, but installed GUI clicks were not exercised. The metadata extent/tolerance is a starting point, not a substitute for source-specific coordinate precision review. Oracle documents `USER_SDO_GEOM_METADATA` as the user-writable metadata view and requires dimension metadata for spatial indexing on versions before automatic metadata registration; tolerance participates in spatial computations and depends on the coordinate system ([metadata views](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/geometry-metadata-views.html), [CREATE INDEX](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/create-index.html), [tolerance guidance](https://docs.oracle.com/en/database/oracle/oracle-database/26/spatl/data-model.html)). MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server spatial-index creation remains unimplemented.
- Evidence: [Oracle GIS roundtrip machine result](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-20261121.json) and [reproducible integration](../tests/integration/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs).

Overwrite registers metadata and creates the SDO domain index on the staged table before swapping it into place. ## 2026-11-22 MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server spatial-index opt-in

- MySQL and MariaDB GIS imports now accept `createSpatialIndex` through Core, CLI, and desktop IPC/UI. This is create-mode only. The staged GeoPackage must have one known positive SRID, at least one feature, and no NULL geometries. Core creates `GEOMETRY NOT NULL SRID <srid>` for MySQL or `GEOMETRY REF_SYSTEM_ID=<srid> NOT NULL` for MariaDB, then creates a single-column SPATIAL index and verifies `information_schema.STATISTICS` reports an R-tree/SPATIAL index on the requested geometry column. MariaDB also receives an enforced `CHECK (ST_SRID(geom) = <srid>)` constraint because its `REF_SYSTEM_ID` metadata alone does not reject geometry values with a different SRID. If a requirement is not met, validation occurs before the target table is created; post-creation failures drop the new table.
- SQL Server geometry and geography imports accept the same opt-in in create mode. Before creating the spatial index, Core changes the staged GeoPackage integer feature ID column to `BIGINT NOT NULL`, adds a clustered primary-key constraint, and verifies it. Geometry uses `GEOMETRY_GRID` with a bounding box derived from the staged XY extent and a 5% margin (with a small scale-aware minimum); geography uses `GEOGRAPHY_AUTO_GRID`. The new index is verified through `sys.indexes` and `sys.index_columns`. The source ID field stays in the destination table and becomes its clustered primary key.
- Live acceptance passed on isolated MySQL 8.4.13, MariaDB 11.4.13, and SQL Server 2022 Developer. MySQL/MariaDB each passed Core and built CLI paths with 2 EPSG:4326 points, Chinese attribute readback, NOT NULL/SRID column checks, and catalog verification. A mixed NULL/non-NULL source was rejected before table creation on both engines. Direct NULL and SRID 3857 writes into the SRID 4326 indexed columns were rejected; MariaDB's CHECK clause was also verified in `information_schema.CHECK_CONSTRAINTS`. SQL Server Core geometry and geography plus CLI geometry each imported 2 EPSG:4326 points; SQL Server catalog checks found a valid SPATIAL index on `geom` and one clustered primary key on the staged feature ID. All seven test tables and temporary files were removed; cleanup errors were zero and `externalDataSent=false`.
- Verification after the final changes: workspace `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, and host-loopback `pnpm test` passed (418 tests passed, 6 existing CLI cases skipped). GUI checkbox/IPC compiled and built but was not clicked in the installed app. Installer launch remains blocked by the Windows system DLL relocation / Electron `0x80000003` issue documented in the preceding package smoke.
- Limitations: spatial-index creation is only for new tables. MySQL/MariaDB reject NULL geometries and require a positive SRID; MySQL enforces SRID on its geometry column, while MariaDB adds a `CHECK` constraint. SQL Server requires the staged integer primary-key field; files without one are rejected. Geometry bounding boxes are based on the current layer extent and may not include future data. Oracle remains create-mode only and requires source-specific review of SDO tolerance. Reproduction: `pnpm exec node tests/integration/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs` and `pnpm exec node tests/integration/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs` (requires authorized local test services and host loopback access). Evidence: [MySQL/MariaDB](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-20261122.json), [SQL Server](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-20261122.json). Official references: [MariaDB ST_SRID](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/geometry-constructors/geometry-properties/st_srid), [MariaDB enforced CHECK constraints](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/data-definition/constraint), [MariaDB spatial index requirements](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-structure/geometry/spatial-index), [MySQL spatial index requirements](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/creating-spatial-indexes.html), [SQL Server spatial index requirements](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/statements/create-spatial-index-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17).

## 2026-11-22 MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server indexed overwrite acceptance

- This section supersedes the preceding spatial-index report's create-only limitation for MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server; that earlier entry records the implementation state before overwrite support.
- Core and CLI now allow `--create-spatial-index` in create and overwrite modes for MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server. Append remains deliberately rejected for these engines. Overwrite creates the replacement table and spatial index in staging, verifies the index on the renamed target (including MariaDB's SRID `CHECK` constraint or SQL Server's clustered primary key), and removes the previous table only after verification.
- If post-swap index verification fails, Core attempts to rename the original table back and clean the staging replacement. If both verification and rollback fail, the error identifies the preserved backup table so the previous contents are recoverable.
- Isolated live acceptance passed for MySQL 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.4.13 through Core and built CLI, and SQL Server 2022 through Core `geometry`, Core `geography`, and built CLI `geometry`. Each path started with an indexed table containing a sentinel geometry, overwrote it with the two synthetic EPSG:4326 points, and verified that the sentinel was gone and the spatial index remained on the new table. MySQL's SRID column restriction and MariaDB's enforced SRID check remained in place. SQL Server spatial-index and clustered-key metadata remained present. An append request with the index option is rejected before writes.
- All temporary tables and files were removed; both successful machine reports say `cleanupErrors=[]`, `externalDataSent=false`, and `temporaryFilesRemoved=true`. A first SQL Server run used the geometry-only `STX` property against a `geography` value; cleanup was independently confirmed, the assertion was corrected to use `STAsText()`, and the rerun passed all three paths. Evidence: [MySQL/MariaDB](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-overwrite-20261122-a2e8385e.json), [SQL Server](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-overwrite-20261122-ace12f15.json). Runners: [MySQL/MariaDB](../tests/integration/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs), [SQL Server](../tests/integration/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs).
- Full workspace verification passed: `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build`, and `pnpm test` (418 passed, 6 existing CLI skips). Desktop checkbox/IPC compiles and supports create/overwrite selection, but no installed-GUI click was performed. MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server indexed append remain unsupported; Oracle indexed append retains its separate metadata/bounds checks. The geometry index's configured bounds are based on staged extent and may not cover future features.

## 2026-11-22 MySQL/MariaDB and SQL Server indexed append acceptance

- Core accepts `createSpatialIndex: true` in append mode for MySQL, MariaDB and SQL Server. Append never creates a second index: it checks destination compatibility before inserts and verifies the existing index after the rows are written.
- MySQL requires a NOT NULL geometry column, matching native `information_schema.COLUMNS.SRS_ID`, and exactly one single-column SPATIAL/R-tree index on the selected geometry column. MariaDB requires the same nullability and index shape, a matching `CHECK (ST_SRID(geom)=n)` expression, and both global and current-session `check_constraint_checks` enabled. MySQL-family append rejects NULL geometry in the source before writes.
- SQL Server requires the selected geometry/geography type, exactly one enabled SPATIAL index, and one clustered primary key on the staged integer GeoPackage feature-ID column. For geometry indexes with finite `sys.spatial_index_tessellations` bounds, the source extent must be inside the existing box; geography and auto-grid metadata without finite bounds skip this comparison. Index and key are checked again after append.
- Isolated live Core and built-CLI acceptance passed on MySQL 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.4.13: create 2 rows, append 2 rows (4 total), then overwrite back to 2. Catalog metadata confirmed that the same index remained after append. A mixed geometry/NULL source was rejected without changing the destination. SQL Server 2022 Core geometry/geography and CLI geometry passed create/append/overwrite. A geometry source outside the existing bbox was rejected before writes and the table remained at 4 rows. All randomized tables and generated temporary files were removed; cleanup error count is zero and `externalDataSent=false`.
- Verification: `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build`, and loopback-authorized `pnpm test` passed (418 passed, 6 pre-existing CLI skips). The unprivileged test run first hit Windows loopback `EACCES`; the authorized local rerun passed. Desktop source and production build enable the append option and show its prerequisites; installed GUI click acceptance remains unverified.
- Evidence: [MySQL/MariaDB](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-append-20261122-44927d72.json), [SQL Server](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-append-20261122-0329d0a5.json). Reproduce with [MySQL/MariaDB smoke](../tests/integration/mysql-family-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs) and [SQL Server smoke](../tests/integration/sqlserver-gis-spatial-index-smoke.mjs).

## 2026-11-22 GIS source-CRS bbox export across five databases

- Added `spatialFilter` to the GIS export contract. Core and CLI accept a source-CRS rectangle; `gis export --bbox minX,minY,maxX,maxY` exposes it in the command line. The desktop GIS page now validates and passes the same rectangle through its export bridge.
- PostGIS exports pass the rectangle to GDAL `-spat`. MySQL, MariaDB, SQL Server, and Oracle stream the source table into a temporary GeoPackage using bounded batches, then GDAL filters features while creating the requested output. The application does not materialize the full table in memory, but these four paths still read and stage all source rows before filtering.
- A live integration initially found that MySQL geographic WKB was returned in the SRS latitude/longitude axis order, so the staged coordinates did not match traditional GIS XY bbox values. MySQL export now calls `ST_AsWKB(..., 'axis-order=long-lat')`; the live MySQL 8.4 test confirms the in-range EPSG:4326 feature is exported. MariaDB retains its one-argument WKB function.
- Live isolated acceptance passed for PostGIS, MySQL 8.4, MariaDB 11.4, SQL Server 2022, and Oracle Free. Core and CLI exports each selected feature ID 17 and excluded ID 18 from a two-point EPSG:4326 fixture. All random database tables and temporary input/output files were cleaned; source data was unchanged and no external data was sent.
- Verification: GIS 44/44, Core 46/46, CLI 27 passed with 6 existing environment skips, desktop 83/83; full workspace `pnpm test` had 421 passed and 6 skipped. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm build` passed. The desktop renderer field/IPC bridge is unit-tested and builds, but installed-GUI click acceptance remains unverified.
- Evidence: [PostGIS Core/CLI export](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/gis-export-spatial-filter-postgis-dd6a96a9.json), [MySQL/MariaDB/SQL Server/Oracle Core/CLI export and cleanup](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/gis-vendor-field-mapping-0078646e.json).

The first 20 text and geometry samples matched for each candidate, source sidecar hashes remained unchanged, temporary exports were removed, and the acceptance schema was dropped.
- The three blocked sources were `F:\temp\海螺沟_物业点.tab` and `F:\temp\grid.tab` (`ReadBytes(): Attempt to read past end of data block` under CP936, CP950, and UTF-8), plus `F:\temp\t_gis_high_speed_segment.tab` (`Too big nCoordDataSize = 30598648` under all three candidates). These are source binary block errors; no database import was attempted for them. The separately user-skipped `F:\GeoData\风景区.zip` member remains skipped.
- This is candidate compatibility evidence only: it does not prove the true source encoding of any layer. No source GIS files were changed and no external data was sent. ## 2026-11-22 Ordinary SQLite SpatiaLite initialization opt-in

- Added a guarded `initializeSpatialite` option from Core through CLI and desktop Electron IPC to the GIS import page. The CLI exposes it as `--initialize-spatialite`; the desktop checkbox is available for SQLite create-mode imports and resets when the active connection changes. It applies only to an existing ordinary SQLite file with no conflicting target table. Append/overwrite, `.gpkg`, non-SQLite routes, and name collisions are rejected before metadata initialization. Without opt-in, ordinary SQLite remains reject-before-write.
- The GDAL SQLite driver's `ogr2ogr -update -dsco SPATIALITE=YES` path ignored the creation option in a direct probe and could write an unregistered geometry BLOB. The implementation instead runs `ogrinfo -update -dialect SQLite -sql "SELECT InitSpatialMetadata(1)"` after guard checks, verifies the result, then uses the SpatiaLite import path. Existing ordinary tables are preserved. If the subsequent feature import fails, the newly initialized metadata can remain; this is included in the CLI report and GUI explanation.
- Synthetic live-GDAL acceptance passed: the default request was rejected and retained the sentinel row; append and target-name collision were rejected before initialization; explicit initialization preserved the existing table, imported two features, registered the geometry and RTree metadata, and exported both features. Existing SpatiaLite and GeoPackage create/append/overwrite checks in the same smoke script also passed. No user source data was used, no source file was modified, and no external data was sent.
- Verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, full workspace `pnpm test` (421 passed, 6 existing CLI skips), and `node tests/integration/sqlite-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs` passed. CLI `gis import --help` shows the option. Machine result: [sqlite-spatialite-gis-acceptance-20261122.json](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/sqlite-spatialite-gis-acceptance-20261122.json); reproducible smoke: [sqlite-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs](../tests/integration/sqlite-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs).
- Installed desktop GUI click acceptance remains unverified because the current Windows package cannot be launched: Windows reports “invalid system DLL relocation” at installer process creation. Source UI/IPC build and unit checks passed, but they do not substitute for GUI acceptance.

## 2026-11-22 Oracle SDO_GEOMETRY Z/M GIS file import

- Oracle `SDO_UTIL.FROM_WKBGEOMETRY` rejected ISO WKB Z/M type offsets (1001/2001/3001) with ORA-13198. Added a bounds-checked WKB walker in the Oracle adapter that recursively rewrites standard ISO dimensional type codes to EWKB Z/M flags, preserves endian and coordinate payloads, strips embedded SRID words into the explicit Oracle SRID bind, and rejects conflicting SRIDs, truncated data, unsupported curve types, or inconsistent multi-geometry dimensions before database writes.
- Oracle Free Core file import passed synthetic XYZ (`SDO_GTYPE=3001`, EPSG:4979, `[104,30,5]`) and a mixed XYM/XYZM GeoPackage (`SDO_GTYPE=3301` with `[104,30,7]`; `SDO_GTYPE=4401` with `[105,31,6,8]`). The built CLI imported the same XYM/XYZM fixtures and preserved the SDO_GTYPE and measures. Read-only DUAL checks confirmed normalized XYZ LineString, XYM Polygon, and XYZM LineString ordinates; unnormalized ISO dimensional WKB failed as expected. XY create/append/overwrite, XY GeoPackage export, and XY spatial-index CLI paths still passed.
- Oracle spatial-index imports remain XY-only. The full staged layer is preflighted before create/append/overwrite writes; a non-XY indexed overwrite was rejected and the original indexed table and VALID domain index remained intact. Oracle file export and existing-table spatial row transfer remain XY-only because the current SDO WKB export path loses Z or cannot generate 4D WKB. Standard WKB geometry type codes 1–7 are handled by the new parser; curve types above 7 remain rejected.
- Verification: full workspace `pnpm test` passed 425 tests with 6 existing CLI skips; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build`, database unit tests (75/75), and the isolated Oracle Core/CLI integration passed. The runners used synthetic features only, cleaned every random Oracle table/index and temporary file, and sent no external data. Machine evidence: [Oracle import roundtrip](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-20261122.json), [read-only WKB probe](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-wkb-dimension-probe-20261122.json); reproducible runner: [oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs](../tests/integration/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs).

## 2026-11-22 Oracle SDO_GEOMETRY Z/M GIS file export

- Oracle's `SDO_UTIL.TO_WKBGEOMETRY` writes XYZ with a zero Z ordinate and rejects XYZM. Export now uses `SDO_CS.MAKE_2D` only for the WKB geometry structure and obtains original coordinate ordinates from `SDO_UTIL.GETVERTICES`. A bounds-checked WKB walker rebuilds ISO XYZ/XYM/XYZM payloads and verifies that every non-empty XY pair and coordinate count exactly matches Oracle's ordered vertex stream; unsupported SDO layouts, WKB types outside 1–7, malformed JSON, and reordered/missing vertices stop the export rather than emit partial geometry.
- GeoPackage layer metadata derives `z`/`m` flags from all non-NULL SDO_GTYPE groups; mixed dimensional layers mark present axes optional. XY continues to use Oracle's ordinary WKB export path. Oracle create/append/overwrite spatial-index paths and existing-table spatial row transfer remain XY-only.
- The isolated Oracle Free integration and built CLI each exported six synthetic rows: XYZ, XYM and XYZM points, an XYZ line, an XYM polygon, and an XYZM line. All coordinates and Z/M ordinates matched exact expected values, all row counts matched, and mixed-layer metadata was `z=2,m=2`. Unit tests cover endian preservation, empty points, polygon rings, nested dimensional MultiLineString, count/XY mismatches, truncation, and unsupported LRS layouts. Other dimensional multi-geometry types are implemented by the checked walker but have not been live verified.
- Verification: `pnpm test:integration:oracle-gis-file-roundtrip`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build`, and full workspace `pnpm test` passed (432 tests passed; 6 existing CLI environment skips). Oracle random tables and temporary input/output files were cleaned, with no external data sent. Installed desktop GUI clicks remain unverified because the Windows installer/runtime startup issue is unresolved.
- Evidence: [Oracle file roundtrip](../.cache/gis-roundtrip/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-20261122.json), [reproducible Core/CLI integration](../tests/integration/oracle-gis-file-roundtrip-smoke.mjs), and [GIS capability matrix](gis-database-capability-matrix.md).
